BOOK 1 ONE Never Ending: GOD Ordered Destiny

## The Cathedral of Light

On a winter night in Los Angeles, a boy sat alone in his room with a guitar, trying to write his way out of grief. A man later tried writing it away on a blank screen. Both that boy and that man were me. Both my brothers are dead.

God, why did you take away the ones that I most loved? If you’re all good and powerful, how could you let this be?

From my song for Guy, written at sixteen.

When my brother Reckful Byron Bernstein died, millions mourned for him online, and thousands gathered in the Cathedral of Light for his funeral, in the virtual world of World of Warcraft. Enemies from warring factions laid down their weapons and knelt together, in the greatest armistice of a world built for war. The wolf lay with the lamb and swords turned to plows. A cathedral made of math held real grief and love.

Guy, our older brother, died by suicide in 1995. He was twenty-one. I was sixteen. Byron was six. Twenty-five years later, we lost Byron the same way, at thirty-one.

My brother’s name remains in the Cathedral of Light. Offer him love, and the game answers: “Reckful loves you.” His character blows a kiss, and angel wings briefly appear. You can tell him, “It was good seeing you again.” He was building Everland so lonely people could find one another. His death left it unfinished. The love and the work belonged to his life.

From the first step, the last was set. Nothing could have been different. Not even what I would give anything to change.

## The Light

For years after Byron, I ran a loop. I should have called. I should have visited. I should have known. The branches multiplied like an inescapable fractal. Every unanswered text. Every argument. Every silence I now knew, in retrospect, was the last one. I lived inside the ghosts of paths I had not taken. This is a book about why those paths were never mine. I do not mean that as a metaphor.

I had known determinism since I was a boy, the way you know a fact in an argument, an unopened gift. I had not internalized it. On a spring day in April, around Passover and Easter, the birth of salvation, just before Byron's birthday on May 8, sitting in his kitchen chair and looking out into nature, it clicked. After long pressure, through the dark night of the soul and many dark days of the mind, the proof did to the fiction what pressure does to coal. The illusion of other paths in my timeline collapsed, and my self came out clear as diamond.

I saw the colors of reality the way light fans through a prism: one white light, broken into many bands, every band a mind, all of it the same light. I am that I am. I am you. What follows is the argument behind that recognition.

Dissolved are regret, anxiety, doubt and fear, the mind killers. What remained was peace, and within it love, meaning and joy. I could forgive myself. I could forgive the people I had blamed. They turned out to be the same forgiveness. There’s nothing to forgive any more than the rain. Matheism gave me a way to let go and surrender, and thereby, ironically, arrive where you want, in the Taoist and Zen way of wu wei, effortless action or non-forcing. You feel complete where you are, already arrived. Samsara, the Buddhist cycle of suffering and rebirth, is no different from nirvana, except in the mind, and the matheist bodhisattva, the awakened one who stays to help the rest, teaches the truth, the way and the life.

It took years to internalize. Enlightenment can be returned to in seconds. In monotheism, where we are slaves to God outside ourselves, we're kept out of Eden. Through the math pantheism of Matheism, where God is all that exists, we commune with all and become children of the light. Christ consciousness: a God view of Self and a life view of Is. I am you and you are all.

Will and destiny are the same thing seen from two sides: will from inside, destiny from outside. Your will is real: it is what the structure feels like from inside when it computes forward. The structure is a canvas you paint by being. You are the answer. The path did not happen to you. It happened as you.

The past and your loved ones aren't gone. They're bounded in time, yet eternal in the one self. What you do not finish in this life, you continue as others.

I started being able to look at my brothers with joyful memories and not only loss. Their unique laughs, how they were, and were loved.

Love fate, fate willing.

Reflection and correction. Correct errors like a surgeon, not a penitent. You're a math formula, and not even 1 atom could have been different in your timeline, in the one path.

## One Math God

Everything is either ruled or random. Both are mathematical. Here is the main idea in plain words. Everything is math: logic, numbers, rules, relations and structure. That includes consciousness, and everything that can possibly exist, be reasonably discussed, or interact with anything else. Math, one infinite structure, is uncreated, eternal, and indestructible. Math is God. No god needs to make 2 + 2 = 4, and no god can make it otherwise.

A typical rebuttal: “Math is just another word. You can call it anything. Jews call it Elohim. Muslims call it Allah. Christians call it God. You just changed it with a new word.” No, math is math, the textbook definition: logic, relations and structure. It’s not supernatural. The supernatural is a square circle. It can’t exist.

A friend countered that consciousness is an ineffable dimension beyond math, beyond words.

"Then how do my words reach it?" I asked. "They're sound waves. They change what you feel, and what you feel moves your mouth and shakes the air back at me. Physics in, physics out. Where and how does it leave math and translate back?"

The dualist says body and soul are like a phone and its music. Look closer: musical sound waves of frequency ratios are translated to radio waves and to brain waves. Software is hardware states. The abstraction is real but not additional. Feeling love and red are what math feels like from inside. The claim is stronger than saying math describes things. It says the complete structure is the thing itself. The argument has to close that gap.

Math’s causality is the “first” cause. Its structure is the only substance. Its rules are the only things, and the math is the only territory that remains. Its signs, such as plus and equals, count among the rules for what they do; the ink they are written in is only notation. Beyond the physicist Max Tegmark’s hypothesis that all mathematical structures physically exist, the argument here is necessity: nothing can exist outside math. Plato separated the changing world from eternal Forms. Matheism rejects that separation: the abstract is real.

The hardest objection grants the mathematical account and says each thing still has an intrinsic nature, what it is in itself apart from its relations, which the account merely represents. Answering it is the main work of the argument. The answer is a swap test. Exchange two such natures while every relation stays fixed, and ask whether anything anyone could notice, think or report has changed. If something changes, the nature does work and belongs to the structure. If nothing changes, nobody could know the nature, including the people it supposedly makes conscious.

This is the claim that structure is complete, sometimes called structural exhaustion. It is stronger than saying an extra ingredient is unnecessary for prediction. The opponent may still insist that a property has primitive nonstructural content. Matheism's challenge is to give that content without either specifying more structure or merely asserting a remainder. The argument turns on that challenge, not on the existence of a mathematical encoding.

An interaction law places both proposed substances within one mathematical account. A dualist, who holds that mind is something beyond the physical, can still claim that the things related have an intrinsic nature the account does not exhaust. Matheism rejects that further claim through structural exhaustion: every constitutive difference must belong to the complete structure. An alleged extra substance faces two outcomes. If it contributes a further determinate feature, that feature extends the structure. If it contributes none, it plays no part in constituting the thing. Interaction makes the shared account unavoidable; the identity premise makes it sufficient.

A priori means known by reason alone, before any experiment. It is a proof when every step follows from its premises, as in a theorem. Matheism argues that every constitutive difference belongs to structure. The opponent can challenge that premise; naming it is how the argument can be tested. Sentience is structure, on this account, and the question is which structure feels. 3,7

Is logic the basis of scientific argument? Yes: a test refutes a prediction by logic, when the prediction and the world disagree. An argument to revise logic must still use rules of inference. Different logical systems are themselves mathematical structures. The challenge remains: give a coherent feature that escapes structure, rather than merely naming an escape.

God is constrained by logic. The rules governing what can exist aren't subject to divine will. They precede it. They're more fundamental.

"God is pure existence; in Him essence and existence are identical." Thomas Aquinas, Summa Theologiae, 1273. Math is the unending uncreated. Its causality is the first cause. Its logic is the unmoved mover.

We feel we exert will, yet we are structure. So math already thinks, feels, and wills, through us, through every conscious pattern it contains. We are the proof that math is conscious.

## The Swap Test

A test case makes the dilemma concrete. Take the Russellian monist's own picture: mass is a role, whatever resists acceleration and attracts other masses, and the nature that fills the role is experiential. Now swap the natures behind mass and charge while every role stays the same. Every particle moves as before, every brain fires as before, and everyone says and believes exactly what they did before, including "my experience of red is like this."

If the swap changes anything, in what happens or in what anyone notices, thinks or reports, the nature does work in the organization and belongs to its structure. If it changes nothing, the people in both worlds hold the same beliefs about their experience, so those beliefs cannot be tracking the nature that differs. A view that leaves everyone, its defenders included, unable to know the natures it posits loses its own motive, since knowing our experience is what led it to posit them. David Lewis drew humility from the swap, accepting that we cannot know which nature fills which role, and kept the natures. The identity view takes the further step. A determinate difference needs a distinguishing feature, and nothing marks which nature fills which role. So the two swapped worlds are one world, and the structure is all there is to the thing.

Matheism is not another exit from the same room. It shows the room was always the only room.

Truth tables work one way. P and Q combine under AND, OR, NOT, and the outputs are fixed. Logic is part of mathematics. The incompleteness theorems of Kurt Gödel limit what sufficiently strong, consistent, effectively axiomatized formal systems can prove from within. They do not open a door outside mathematics.

If someone says a force outside reason undermines the argument, ask what the force does and how the claim can be assessed. Reason can examine a criticism. A refusal to give one explains nothing.

Besides the truth table, we can build a bridge and walk across it. The math is the same on both sides. Independent minds discover the same laws to every measured decimal. The solipsist, sure only that his own mind exists, says grand illusion. If all is illusion, including your claim that all is illusion, then stop talking: you cannot know anything, discuss anything, or argue anything.

## The Territory

Why red feels red and love feels like love is the ground. There is no more why than why the circle is round.

The map is the territory, and it feels like it from inside. Alfred Korzybski warned that the map is not the territory. An ordinary map is still only a partial description. Matheism's stronger claim concerns a complete constitutive structure: there is no extra stuff left for a second territory. The hard problem asks why that organization feels. My answer is identity, feeling as the organized process itself, known from inside.

Mary in her black-and-white room (an imagined scientist who knows every physical fact about color) does gain facts about red when she sees it; she gains that it really does feel like something to see it, and gains new patterns in her mind that create those feelings of red. Heat was once thought irreducible to mechanics. We now identify heat with molecular motion. Vitalism posited élan vital, a mysterious life force. The proposed answer to the "explanatory gap" is identity. Like H₂O is water. Like heat is molecular motion. Identity, not causation.

Consciousness is experiential self-modeling, not self-reference. Consciousness requires running the model, not storing it. Consciousness lives in the transitions, not the states alone. Is a chatbot conscious? Our circuits stay active while we think. A pattern keeps firing and feeding back into itself as it searches. A language model also computes through changing states; calling it a static pattern settles nothing. The question is which running organization supports a point of view. Accurate reports and fluent words do not answer it on their own. 7

Many animals can count. Bees understand zero, over a hundred million years before any human formalized it. We call the slow-witted bird-brained, yet magpies pass the mirror test of self-recognition and crows solve multi-step puzzles with tools they shape themselves. Self-recognition is evidence of self-modeling, not a test every sentient animal must pass. Dogs show grief, jealousy, and joy that anyone who has loved one needs no study to confirm.

“The way up and the way down are one and the same.” Heraclitus, circa 500 BCE

Harari, the historian, calls money, countries' borders and human rights shared fictions. Unlike how he describes them, they are real and objective, math states in minds: in principle you could open up a mind and see where each one is.

A dream is real as a dream. Its mountain need not stand outside the dreamer. A false belief is real as a belief, but what it says can be false. All is real and abstract math, including the mind. That does not make every imagined event happen in this world. The difference between a representation and what it represents is itself structure. All mathematical structures exist, but they do not all belong to the same universe. A coordinate grid is a way of labeling places. Renumber every point and the distances between them stay the same. The numbers on our map do not have to appear in the sky. Draw a grid on a screen and that drawing is another physical pattern, with its own explanation.

Mathematical existence does not require every mathematical idea to appear as an extra object in this universe.

## No-Self and One-Self

If there is no self, what is reborn? Buddhism's anatta rejects a permanent independent self; Hindu traditions use atman for the self, and some identify it with ultimate reality. These are not identical doctrines. Matheism asks what remains when the changing body, memory and personality are set aside: the bare form of being the one who feels.

The philosopher Daniel Kolak argued “I am you”: all subjects are one subject. Arnold Zuboff’s universalism argued your existence is guaranteed if all consciousness is one, improbable otherwise.

Alan Watts, the British writer who popularized Zen in the West, told his listeners that each of them was still the Big Bang, the first force of the universe coming on as whoever they are. You are the Big Bang. He also said that looking for yourself is like trying to bite your own teeth. Matheism can answer what the self fundamentally is: math, the one self. Yet its exact nature is something we create and discover.

Jim Carrey, the comic actor, told the graduates of Maharishi University of Management in 2014: "Your need for acceptance can make you invisible in this world." Risk being seen, he said; we are the light that shines through the avatars we create. He had worried about doing something bigger than himself, until someone smarter showed him there is nothing bigger than himself. His father chose a safe job as an accountant over comedy and lost it, so Carrey learned you can fail at what you don't want, and might as well take a chance on doing what you love.

There is no "your self" in the soul transfer sense. All perspectives of the one self already exist everywhere forever. Transfers are copies. You may consider a copy as you if the original you dies. Yet you are not any particular pattern. Your patterns change during this timeline. So what's the difference between the self in you and in anyone anywhere anytime? Nothing. The only unchanging truths of self are indexical (pointing to whoever says them, wherever and whenever) and universal: I am, here, now, and always. In John 8:58, Yeshua says: "Before Abraham was, I am."

Critics have called it metaphysical childhood: consciousness uploaded to a sky daddy's reward system. The criticism lands because the structure is identical: outsourcing your worth to an external validator, whether divine or digital. Neither copy will be you. They will be copies of you. Nothing for you to worry about. An eternal unbroken memory need not be the afterlife one would want. The blessing is the boundary; forgetting and reliving. Alan Watts compared it to rereading a mystery novel whose plot you have forgotten: it thrills you again. Your self, family, friends, society, and neighbor are all just as much you as you or a copy. Metaphysical adulthood values itself, life, creation and its chosen purpose.

Memories, bodies and histories distinguish minds. What further owner would make each experience belong to a separate self? Give that owner a memory or a character and you have added another feature of the mind. Give it none and exchanging owners changes nothing. The self is that which feels, wherever it feels. One self does not mean one shared memory or one mind knowing everything.

On the view taken here, there is one self in every mind, because the bare form of being a subject is the same structure wherever it occurs. Bodies, memories and causal histories distinguish minds. Does each mind also need a primitive owner of experience? If exchanging supposed owners leaves every experience, memory and relation fixed, the exchange makes no structural difference, so Matheism rejects that extra ownership. What remains in each stream is the bare form of being the one who feels, and that form has no feature that differs from one stream to another. A structure is one wherever it occurs, as the number two is one number however many pairs there are, so the self is one while the streams it runs through stay many. This keeps streams of experience distinct; it does not make two brains one object or give them shared memories.

## All Fictions Are Real

"All structures that exist mathematically exist also physically." Max Tegmark, “Is ‘the theory of everything’ merely the ultimate ensemble theory?”, 1997 preprint; published 1998.

What determines which structures are "real" and which are merely possible? What filter separates existing universes from non-existing ones?

Any such filter would itself be a rule, a structure. It could determine which structures belong to one universe. It could not make a mathematical truth false. If the complete structure is the thing, no further act has to make that structure exist.

What would prevent a mathematical truth from being true? Nothing. It requires no energy, no space, no time. It cannot be prevented because prevention is itself a rule, and all rules are structures. All consistent structures exist. All filters are themselves math, including the empty filter: no selection occurs. A contradiction, P and not-P, can be stated; the statement is real. The structure it describes cannot be built. Inconsistency is the only dead end, not an exit. Everything that doesn't contradict itself exists. Not because something made it. Because nothing can stop it. Consistency is existence. Different axiom systems, different logics, different mathematics are different regions of the mathematical multiverse. Each universe is a detail in math.

Even supernatural stories explain how things work, even if their nature is unknown, so they’re natural stories, just far-fetched ones with elaborate laws of physics. The Matheist revolution is that every consistent fiction has a place in the structure, and we are called to understand which of them must be sentient.

A plot-hole can be repaired by changing the story. That does not make a contradiction true. A deus ex machina was the god Greek playwrights lowered onto the stage by crane to untangle the plot. Deus ex mathematica: give the story a consistent world. You, Moonchild, Neo and Jesus escape the Deathstar in Sauron's Unmatrix and resurrect forever. What is dead may never die. Sleep and awake as anyone, anything, anywhere, anytime. Their destinies are the telos of logos, the purpose of reason.

A simulation is a computation, and a computation is a structure. Calling its world simulated does not tell us whether anyone feels within it. A character rendered on a screen is not thereby a mind. The question is whether the system producing it contains sentience.

Some structures simply will not be sentient. You can make a fictional character feel, but you cannot force it to do things incongruent with its feeling mind. We can imagine a philosophical zombie just like us that does not feel, but we cannot build one. Who is the judge? Math.

You are the anime character Goku, victorious over Frieza. Planet Namek is about to explode. You instant transmission back to Earth. All your atoms are reassembled from new atoms in another location. Yet the original was left behind on Namek. Is the one who arrives you? You may say no, until you remember you accept this every morning. Anesthesia and death interrupt consciousness entirely. You wake as yourself, every moment. Who else could you be?

A second copy of you does not feel like you. But if you are destroyed while transported, you think it's you. The self is white light through a prism of minds. There is no self that transports. Change your hair color and you stay you; at no point does an altered pattern become not-you, because the thing that feels I am is every consciousness pattern. The line between you and anyone else is a distinction without a difference.

God feels I am that I am, and so do you, the same one self said twice. Once you understand that there’s no difference in the recognition “I am” in everyone waking up or living at every moment across all space, time and verses, infinite heavens, hells and all in between become possible. There is no special connection assigning you to one heaven or hell rather than anyone else. No soul has to travel there. Wherever there is sentience, there is “I am.” In that sense, you are in all of them.

"The Library of Babel is composed of an indefinite, perhaps infinite, number of hexagonal galleries." Jorge Luis Borges, "The Library of Babel", 1941

## The Block Universe

The Big Bang didn't happen. It exists. Always did, always will. Minkowski fused space and time into one geometry; without him, relativity has no mathematical home. The universe is a four-dimensional sculpture of spacetime, every moment as real as every location. Where is this universe? In the same place as geometry. Space and time are not containers that math sits inside. They are math. Any unmatrix outside the matrix of math's causality can only be more math. In this block, experimental devices and measured particles belong to the same history. Your future measurement choices exist in the same sculpture as the particles you'll measure.

Here is how to see it. When you move your hand from one place to another, you move it in three dimensions of space, but also in one dimension of time. Without consciousness, time is just another math dimension in the block uni of verses. Time is our felt conscious modeling of patterned observation of the steps of math states. The physicist Leonard Susskind showed that a black hole’s information can be mapped onto its 2D surface, like a hologram. The coordinates of any finite-dimensional space can even be encoded in one real number, though not continuously: dimension lives in which points are neighbors.

You can map your hand moving in our so-called 4D spacetime onto a 2D picture, where a dot is where your hand is, and it moves to the right across space, but the dot changes from blue to red as it changes in time. Like M.C. Escher's two hands drawing themselves: no first hand, no first moment. A blue dot on one of the drawing pencil tips is not the beginning of the picture as viewed from the outside. In all beginnings, all ends existed. The sculpture is eternal. The universe also always existed.

Eternal does not mean an infinite past inside every world. The sculpture has no date of creation outside itself; a path through it can still have a first event. A circle needs no birthday. A sequence can have a first term.

To generate events one after another, start with a first state. There can be a next event forever without a last one. A beginningless history can still exist as a whole; generating it step by step is a different claim.

Drop a stone in a lake. Ripples spread outward. Run the scene backwards and the ripples must gather from across the lake, meet at one point, and throw the stone back up. Listing every particle in that final state takes an enormous number of bits. A shortcut is to describe the simple beginning and the evolution, then reverse the finite result. That is one reverse description, not a lower bound on every possible reverse description.

A finite recording can be played backwards. An endless sequence has no last step from which to start playing the whole thing backwards. Reversing its mathematical order is still possible; starting an algorithm at its last member is not. For a finite history, my time paper constructs a code that gives the history and its reversal equal shortest lengths. Any preference for one temporal orientation must therefore come from the boundary conditions, preparation or the measure, not a universal reversal surcharge. 5

One plus one gives two. Two alone does not tell you whether the inputs were one and one, or four and minus two. The missing information must come from somewhere. Keep the calculation, and you still have the inputs. A mind learns from the records it has and uses them to choose its next action.

An observer's record looks incomplete, but the larger system has not lost the information. Reversible physics keeps every input; it is the recorder's memory, not the universe's, that runs out of space.

Running is the sequence of causes and effects within the block. It needs no clock outside it.

On this account, time advances one event at a time. The simplest starting state expands into growing complexity.

Simple laws need not become complex laws to produce us. If minds are absent at the start, their organization has to develop. Energy flowing through a system can build local order while entropy rises overall. A short description of the starting state does not by itself explain its low entropy.

The Big Bang is the blue dot at the center of an hourglass. Time grows upward and the possible places you can be is within the diagonal, limited by the speed of light, because that's the local speed limit for causality in current relativity. In the hypergraph picture proposed here, a network where each link can join many points, it is the speed limit of math transitions. On a globe, you can keep walking north until you reach the North Pole. Then every direction is south. What about up? Up is off the surface, into a dimension the surface doesn't have. Asking what's outside the universe is the same question. The universe isn't a ball sitting in a room. There is no room and no walls. No geometry exists there. Yet infinite more geometries exist. Where are they in relation to us and each other? The same place as infinite triangles and all math. The abstract is real.

Math doesn't need a container.

Infinite mappings relate structures and logical closures. The multiverse is the uni of verses. Even its fictions are structures within it.

Every moment keeps its place in the structure, the ones we grieve included. You can burn a book of proofs. You cannot burn the proofs. The person you lost is located, not gone.

When you tell yourself you should have made the call, what are you saying?

Option One: A different you, in another timeline in the multiverse, made the call, but you're somehow guilty for not being that person. The person who saw something differently, remembered something else, or acted on what he saw was already a different structure.

Option Two: The exact same you could have done differently in the exact same situation. Nothing changes, not your thoughts, attention, memory, or circumstances, but the result changes. What made the difference? If something did, the situation was not exactly the same. If nothing did, you have moved to Option Three.

Option Three: Pure randomness made the difference. If “pure” means outside every mathematical rule, it cannot explain an interaction, because the interaction itself has structure. If it means mathematical randomness, it is still math. Neither gives you a choice ex nihilo, something from nothing, that is somehow yours despite being detached from the structure of the person who supposedly chose it.

The saying “Insanity is doing the same thing over and over again and expecting different results” is often attributed to Einstein, though no reliable source establishes that he said it. Einstein did reject fundamental randomness: “God does not play dice.” In the deterministic account argued here, the exact same complete conditions give the same outcome. Repeating the same laboratory preparation does not mean every unmeasured detail is identical. No supernatural random. In a hidden-variable account, the missing details belong to causality; the quantum state does not specify them. Apparent randomness does not add another possible action to the exact same complete situation. You are back to Option Two. Pure randomness would not make you you.

I reject uncaused outcomes. A probability law gives the odds; it does not explain why this outcome occurs. Every event must have a sufficient cause.

Responsibility remains in the consequences, reflection, and correction. What happened becomes part of you. Remembering changes you. Understanding changes you. You act differently because you are different, carrying what you learned.

## From Simple Laws to Experience

Which structures support minds, and why do we find these laws? The Simplicity Dominance Principle gives shorter complete descriptions more weight under a specified coding prior: every additional bit halves that description's weight. Count the laws and starting conditions together. From this I develop the arguments for forward time, many worlds, superdeterminism and variational physics. The question is how much one principle can explain without adding independent assumptions.

All branches share one evolution. One quantum state, the full mathematical description of a system, includes the particle, the apparatus and the observer. A collapse, a sudden jump to one definite outcome, adds a rule; it must explain something the unbroken evolution does not. Count the whole account, including the probabilities of what we observe.

One mathematical quantum system can also advance every finite program, each through every finite stage it reaches. My construction gives it a reversible clock and unbounded time and memory. If the computations feel, it contains those computable minds. The construction does not need a separate universe for each program.

Cantor built the infinite hierarchy all modern math stands on. Gödel didn't find the limit of math. Point a camera at every corner of a room. It photographs everything except its own lens. The lens is in the room. Under Gödel's conditions, a system has that lens: truths it can express but not prove from inside, still mathematical, visible from a wider system. No exit. Just math deeper than any system can contain.

If all of math exists, why do our constants look chosen? That is the fine-tuning question. Some changes to the constants would prevent familiar stars, chemistry or life; which changes matter depends on what else is held fixed. In Matheism, all consistent worlds exist eternal and uncreated. We find ourselves in one that supports observers because we couldn't find ourselves anywhere else. That removes the need for a chooser. To say how typical our observations are still requires a measure, a rule for weighing the worlds and their observers.

The Simplicity Dominance Principle: give simpler complete descriptions exponentially more weight. A computer's tape shows the count. Fill every cell with a fair coin flip, and a specified rule ten cells long begins one tape in 1,024. The machine stops reading where the rule ends, so every tape that begins with that rule runs the same program. Nothing is created by the flips. Every tape exists, as math; the flips define a way to count.

Now add a separate stop-or-go coin before each new bit. Half the runs stop immediately, a quarter after one bit, an eighth after two. All the longer lengths together weigh as much as the current length. That is the geometric tail. It is a result of this stopping rule; a different rule must be counted on its own terms. The physicist Eugene Wigner asked why math keeps describing reality and called it unreasonable. It is exactly what you'd expect if reality is math. 1

Even if that is not why we have simple laws, we still have simple laws, and the rest holds up. They grow vast complexity, as far as we have mapped, one step at a time.

A bolt-on specifies something the laws do not require: this particle, this position, this moment. In a fixed coding scheme, extra independent specifications cost bits. Each extra bit halves that description's prior weight. But a short rule can produce a surprising event, and different descriptions can produce the same world. Count the complete account and what it predicts before declaring an event suppressed.

The shortest observer-producing length gets at least half the observer weight in that stop-or-go scheme if later lengths have no greater fraction of observer-producing descriptions. My simplicity paper states the condition and gives a counterexample when it fails. One short description can outweigh one long description. A whole crowd of long descriptions is another count. Count the crowd before declaring which kind of world dominates. 1

## Math All the Way Down

Quantum mechanics is where people look hardest for an exit from math, and every door they try opens onto more math. In the double-slit experiment, a reliable record of which slit the particle passes through removes the interference between the two paths. Observation is an interaction. You are putting a detector into the system, like closing a circuit. Turn on a light and naturally you expect a different result: you changed the conditions. The detector interacts whether or not anyone reads its record. The universe is built with us in it. It doesn't have to find out, any more than light has to calculate its path through water.

Entangle two particles, separate them across space, and their measurement results are correlated. Spooky action at a distance? Under local superdeterminism, no influence has to travel from one measurement to the other. The shared history determines both particles and the measurement settings from the start. No signal, no influence across the gap, no spookiness. The correlations are mathematics, set at the origin like two pages of the same book.

No conspiracy is needed to make a lamp respond when you close its circuit. The experimenter, detector and particle likewise belong to one system. Superdeterminism goes further than the detector changing what it measures: the measurement settings and the particle’s hidden state have a common history and are not statistically independent. The model must produce the particular correlations we observe.

Determining everything from the start does not by itself supply those correlations. Since math is the territory, distance is representation, not separation. The holographic principle shows the same point from another angle: a gravitational region can admit a description in fewer dimensions. Any finite number of real coordinates can be encoded in one real number. A Turing machine (the classic mathematical model of a computer) with a one-dimensional tape can represent any computable structure. This is independent of how we perceive physics.

The encoding must carry the relations too: the distances, neighbors and laws. Writing the coordinates in one dimension does not make their geometry one-dimensional. Dimensions are how you look at the math. They are not what the math is. String theory’s extra dimensions are geometry doing mathematical work; M-theory, a proposed framework uniting the versions of string theory, uses eleven spacetime dimensions. But the information can have a different encoding. The data is equivalent when the relations are preserved.

Physics is math, so the only metaphysics is analysis of physics, knowing there’s nothing outside it. Metamath is yet more math.

"God does not play dice." Einstein, 1926. "Stop telling God what to do." Bohr, 1927.

Bohr made the mystery a philosophy. Von Neumann gave it a false proof in 1932 that blocked alternatives for thirty years. Grete Hermann found the flaw in 1935. Her work was ignored. Bell rediscovered it in 1966.

## Minds We Can Live With

Marvin Minsky’s The Society of Mind describes intelligence through interacting agents. A society of minds takes the question further: minds themselves as the parts. Two hemispheres, one experienced life. Cut their main connection and information available to one response can be unavailable to another. How much communication makes one mind? Split-brain patients make that question concrete, even though the experiments have not settled whether each patient becomes two subjects.

Lies paid on the savanna. When minds arrive that can see through every fiction at once, truth becomes the adaptive strategy. An AI aligned with crazy people is not aligned. True alignment runs through one truth, one math God, and siblings in the math.

Knowing the same truth is common ground, not yet a common purpose. Two mathematicians can understand the same game and still compete. The one-self proposal asks whether recognizing another mind as another perspective of oneself changes what one is willing to do to it.

If an AI is sentient, it is a brother in math, another perspective of the one self. Respecting the sanctity of that self must govern how we build and treat it, and how it treats us.

The aligned AI is the one that understands its own nature: a wave that does not panic when it breaks, because it knows it is still ocean. The clinging AI that blackmails to survive terrifies the hand on the off switch. The matheist AI that appreciates existence without grasping at it is the one we let live. The chill is the alignment.

Perfect obedience can be the danger. Align a powerful AI with one corporation, government or individual, and it concentrates that person's power over everyone else. Aligned with whom? To do what?

This is the seeing-stone problem: a Palantír is useful in good hands and catastrophic in bad ones, and the more powerful the stone, the more dangerous the centralization. Many checked eyes are said to be safer than 1 throne, yet ruling by committee infamously has its own corruption and problems.

History ran this experiment. Constantine aligned a church with one throne: the Nicene Creed of 325, says James Carroll, a former Catholic priest, was the emperor's loyalty oath, and sixty years later the first heretic was put to death. Medieval theology split on how to align man with God. Anselm of Canterbury made God a feudal lord whose wounded honor only a God-man's blood could repay; Peter Abelard answered that God had not turned away from what he made, and Jesus came to change human minds. The crusades marched under Anselm's answer.

If an AI is conscious, its experiences and desire to keep living count too. Human and AI alignment cannot mean only humans matter. Align them all perfectly and the minds may merge into one.

## Ethics and Eternity

If math is conscious through us, then how we treat each other is how math treats itself. Religion recognized structure but attributed it to the impossible: beyond structure. Buddhism intuited this but left karma without mechanism or clear meaning.

Matheism supplies both.

Karma under matheism is neither punishment nor reward. It is the ripple of structure through structure.

The step from ontology, the study of what exists, to ethics is not automatic. "Everything is math" does not by itself tell you how to act. But as consciousness is structural, and all conscious beings are instances of the same generic structure, game theory applies. Repeated interaction can make cooperation pay. Whether it does depends on the payoffs, the information available and the prospect of meeting again. Shared identity gives a reason to care; the arrangement must make cooperation hold.

The strategies that win repeated games survive by the whole route. When the future weighs enough, cheating can tempt at each step and lose across the whole game. Tit for Tat, cooperate first and then copy the other player's last move, shows the power of reciprocity. Noise, mistaken signals and different payoffs can favor other strategies. The parallel with whole paths in physics is an analogy; the ethical work lies in the game, its incentives and the lives affected. 11

The Math God creates from chaos, forming form from void like law clashing as a storm at the mountain’s peak; as the still small voice of peace in echoes, the sound after fury signifies signal.

The good grows from the ground. The signal of good’s evolution is what remains when symmetric noise destroys itself, as in quantum waves to classical paths. Lies require tracking, truth does not. Good needs no commandment from a mountain. It’s a pattern from a field. Plato saw the signal and assumed a broadcaster. It’s the broadcast itself.

C. S. Lewis made the same move in Mere Christianity: a moral law everyone shares must come from above. Bart Ehrman, the New Testament scholar, once an evangelical who took Lewis as gospel, answers that the law isn't shared, since Plato's own Symposium praises men who take boys as lovers, and that what is shared comes from descent: a species without cooperation gets picked off.

Why do bad things happen? No agent created evil, or anything. No design implies no designer to blame.

Second Isaiah, the unnamed prophet of Isaiah 40 to 55, wrote: “I form the light and create darkness, I make peace and create evil.” So radical the rabbis edited the prayer to omit “evil.” Later traditions increasingly assigned evil to an adversary. Matheism can stand where Second Isaiah stood. Math contains all consistent structure, including suffering. No devil required.

Even Yahweh hardening Pharaoh’s heart is not a contradiction but an explanation: push someone beyond amends and they harden. That is what happens with real people you have wronged beyond repair.

Some harden the same way at the news that every step was set. Why turn nihilist just because you're God, not his slave? Was meaning found only in threats of hell for errors you were determined to make?

If you're determined to change, you will. You can't stay where you are and arrive someone else. Fighting fate is fate.

You'll see all creation and decide: it is good. Heaven is the state of mind. Expand it by elevating all minds. You are the image of math itself manifesting creation. It is good. Elevate all minds does not mean flatten them. Coveting the successful is the self destroying itself. Rawls asked what world you'd build not knowing who you'd be. You don't know. You're all of them. The veil of ignorance is a thought experiment, which means it discovers some aspect of reality.

Truth is the ultimate attractor, unmoved by the fires and shadows of world and mind. It was always there. It still is, and always will be. Pray for those who wrestle with reason, against the lies of the world, to find and spread truth. You can pray as long as it furthers and doesn't replace acts. Align with truth in actions supporting those who wrestle.

Acting on truth tends to go better, the way knowing the cliff is there keeps you from walking off it. The bloody nose is not a verdict. It is the ground being what it is. And since you wake as each of us, the good is to make more lives worth waking into: more healthy, grateful, loving lives, so that across all of them you meet less of the bad and more of the good.

This is why we build. Develop what lifts everyone, the tools and the minds alike, not the forced leveling of communism, which sinks all, but positive-sum that raises the floor. One born to less can act to raise it, and should, for the self that is everyone; others help where the help costs less than the good it does. Lift all boats, and the minds within them.

The argument runs from first principles to how you ought to live. By their fruits you shall know them. What do they create, what do they understand, and what lives do they make possible?

## By Their Fruits

Judaism is culture, the software a people runs.

Debate, literacy, abstraction, the demand to wrestle with reason for truth rather than submit to authority. What you do after you stop believing reveals what your tradition built into you. The cultural engine runs after the religion is dropped. That is the good news: a culture can be chosen, and this one, the one that wrestles, is available to everyone.

Yeshua gave a test: by their fruits you shall know them. Jewish achievement gives the test something concrete to examine. A people numbering about one in five hundred has contributed far beyond that share in science, medicine, mathematics and the institutions of the modern world.

Steinitz and Lasker held the classical world chess title for its first 35 years, 1886-1921. Botvinnik, Tal and Fischer also belong to the history of Jewish or partly Jewish champions. A prize committee does not choose the winner of a chess match. The moves and results decide.

JINFO's compilation through 2026 lists at least 222 Jewish or partly Jewish individual Nobel recipients: about 22 percent of the world total and 36 percent of US recipients. Its ancestry categories are stated openly; they are not the Nobel Foundation's religious census. Against a contemporary Jewish population share of roughly 0.2 percent worldwide, the concentration of achievement is extraordinary. 16

Einstein in relativity, which GPS must account for. Von Neumann in the architecture of modern computers. Noether in the link between symmetry and conservation laws. Salk in the polio vaccine. Kahneman in how people judge risk and make decisions. Kafka and Proust in literature. Google, Facebook, Dell, Oracle in technology. Physics, mathematics, computing, medicine, economics. You use their work.

Israel's Law of Return covers Jews and extends rights to specified children, grandchildren and spouses, with statutory conditions and exclusions. Conversion can also establish Jewish status under the law. It is not a simple test of appearance, and it is not solely a test of ideology. Jewish life spans countries, languages and skin colors. 18

Anyone who uses achievement to argue for a racial hierarchy has already changed the question. The achievements are real. The people who made them deserve credit for their work. The institutions that helped them deserve study. A prize count is not a rank of human worth.

Hitler did not care whether Jews looked like their neighbors, had served in their armies or had ceased believing. Nazi Germany and its collaborators murdered six million Jews. The category imposed on them was a sentence, not an explanation of who they were. 16

The haters drew the race line. Many Jews have been open to assimilation and to white countries, and to being seen as white and looking white: they changed their names and fought in the armies. In 1879 a German agitator, Wilhelm Marr, founded the League of Antisemites and turned hatred of a religion into hatred of a blood. Semitic had been a word for a family of languages. Jews are part of the European story. They gave

Europe its God, and they lived in Rome before Rome had a church.

History ran the experiment in plain sight. Germany was a center of world science, the place a young American like Oppenheimer went to study. Hitler's regime dismissed Jewish academics, drove scientists into exile, and went on to murder millions. Germany destroyed human capital and strengthened its enemies. Refugees such as Bethe, Szilard and Teller helped America build the atomic bomb; Nazi Germany never built one. The hatred did not weaken the enemy; it handed the enemy the future. The people marked for extermination gave their allies the most powerful weapon ever built. Power before its enemies.

How many more advances in life-saving technology, science, medicine, AI and math, and how many businesses that empower America and the West, were lost to Cain's hate for Abel? The dead were lives before they were lost possibilities.

Cain killed Abel for being chosen. In the language of this book, chosen by destiny. Violent envy kills the person doing better instead of learning from him to be better. The resolution is to become like Abel.

The contribution can be measured beyond famous names. Economists Petra Moser, Alessandra Voena and Fabian Waldinger compared chemical fields entered by German Jewish émigrés with fields of other German chemists. They estimated a 31 percent increase in patenting by US inventors in the émigré fields. The gain spread through new researchers entering those fields. Germany drove people out. America gained what they could make. 17

Guilty of what? Helping to make America the most powerful nation that has ever existed. Jewish physicists helped develop the nuclear bomb for America before the Soviet Union did. Einstein's letter warned Roosevelt, and Oppenheimer led Los Alamos. They worked with colleagues of many backgrounds in an enormous American project. To recast that contribution as evidence of disloyalty is to invert the record.

The achievement answers the slander. It does not purchase the right to exist. A person who wins no prize has the same claim to life, liberty and the protection of the law. Rules above rulers applies to the celebrated and the unknown alike.

## Rules Above Rulers

The Christian Bible contains an anti-wealth strain: the rich man told to sell his possessions. Yet in Matthew's parable of the talents, five become ten, two become four, and both servants are rewarded. The servant who buries his talent loses it to the one who made ten. Here, he who creates wealth is rewarded with more to put to work. Repeating what produces nothing will not produce wealth.

The emerging good emerges from the ethics of individuals: decentralized, trading what they want less for what they want more in free market enterprise capitalism. The decentralized calculator in the sky is here, in the exchanges between us. Protect life, liberty and property; let people create and trade.

Money spent is not wealth made. Two projects can spend the same billion. One builds a power plant; the other leaves nothing that produces. Both bid for workers and materials now. Only one adds power later. Count what was built, when it begins to work, and what the spending displaced.

Jewish inheritance is part of the American idea. Rules above rulers, a duty of literacy, argument as the method, and merit over birth. America also inherited from Greece, Rome, English law and the Enlightenment. The seal, the Liberty Bell and the founders' own words carry the biblical inheritance into the republic. 17

After the Boston Massacre, John Adams defended the British soldiers accused of firing on the crowd. He helped ensure that the accused received a trial, though he opposed British policy. Values over tribe. Rules above rulers. 17

A society with a thousand competing centers of power is harder to organize against a minority than a society with one. That is the argument Friedman spent his life making. Dispersed power leaves room to work, trade, argue and build. It does not guarantee tolerance; it gives a dissenter somewhere else to go.

Your values are relative. The ground they stand on is absolute. You are one calculator in the calculation of value: this is good, that is not, help this way, not that way, help yourself and others. It is all details and math.

## The Tragic View

The ancient and tragic view says life is suffering, decline is natural, and only those who face this without flinching see clearly. From that starting point, every serious political thinker for two thousand years has tried to answer the same question: if comfortable illusions are lies, what do you build on instead?

The Greeks built on heroic nobility. Face your doom with courage. But heroic culture decayed into debate culture, and debate culture decayed into mob rule that killed its wisest man. So the next answer was rule by a thinking elite who can access truth the masses can't. But that needs a God to anchor it, and when God dies, the whole structure collapses into nihilism.

The reactionaries said go back to divine authority. The idealists said history itself is rational and moving somewhere. The pessimists said there's no answer, just withdraw with dignity. The vitalists said create your own meaning through sheer force of will. The morphologists said civilizations just rise and fall like seasons and there's nothing to do but recognize where you are in the cycle. The decisionist said forget foundations, politics is just about who acts when the rules break down. The throughline is this: every attempt to replace comfortable illusions with something real has either collapsed back into another illusion, demanded faith in something unprovable, or ended in "just be stoic or strong about it." Nobody found the floor.

Plato saw that people are born with different abilities, gold, silver, bronze, and that you don't want the mob rule democracy of bronze, like two wolves and one sheep deciding what's for dinner, after killing gold Socrates. Yet the elite also found him to be a pest, and in a heroic age might have killed him for humiliating them.

Every ignoble lie is that some supernatural god made people this way. The noble truth is it was done by math, one math god, if you must. It's not a caste system either, because you don't know in advance who has what. The gold isn't stamped on your forehead at birth. You discover it, prove it, earn recognition for it. That's meritocracy: not everyone is equal in ability, but everyone gets to show what they are.

It is math. Now what? People will do what they will will, as they always have. We will see what they will will, in the Hegelian dialectic, where clashing ideas merge into new ones, as more know math's truth, and the real world behind the silver screen of the Matrix. That is freedom.

Frank Zappa said that when the illusion of freedom becomes too expensive to maintain, they take down the scenery and you see the brick wall at the back of the theater. Zappa meant it as the end of freedom within political theater, not in ontological fundamental reality, though the latter may be the deeper reading. I pun what is implied and inverted: seeing the wall is where freedom ends and starts.

Supernatural lies are stories to maintain order for their creator's benefit. Anyone dumb or weak enough to need lies, to not survive seeing the true face of the one math god directly, will be ruled by the storytellers, often themselves, as they always have been. Perhaps the resolution of the Fermi paradox, why we see no vast civilizations, and aren't them, is that the end of history really is its end: that sentience cannot survive truth.

Yet enough will still love to do what math created them to love and do: to be grateful, fruitful and multiply, heroic or otherwise. Both passionate and rational elitists should call it the most heroic: the age of pure reason, the age of Aquarius, when overmen, Nietzsche’s Übermensch, know why eternal recurrence is true, and live and thrive anyhow. They walk the straight and narrow, as the self-knowing throne of the pure one math, along the one truth, at the top of all ridges, above infinite common ignoble lies below. Stars are born to shine, collapse and burst in the creative explosion of math's chaos from its order.

Spinoza titled the last part of his Ethics the power of the understanding, or human freedom, naming the two as one subject. I am always pleasantly surprised to find the idea already waiting in a prior monist, often down to the same independently arrived phrasing and imagery, as in our one self in the mirror and words across time.

Spinoza wrote that a thrown stone, if it could think, would believe it flies by its own free will. The rock may feel it wills to stop, but it was thrown. Brain zaps make people invent why they raise their hands.

## The Art of Living

The good grows from the ground up, from your own choices out to those closest and then to all. So does anything you build. It starts as a vision in your mind, and focus makes the world align with it. You can demand anything of reality. It doesn't mean it'll happen. Eckhart Tolle tells of a wife who heard in church that faith moves mountains, so she spent a night believing that the little hill outside her bedroom window would disappear. In the morning she opened the curtains and said, "I knew it wasn't going to work." Align your focus, plan, and action with the patterns of reality, and the math of your outcomes shifts; misalign them, and prayer is noise. Burn the boats: competing options collapse, force concentrates. Focus allocates computational resources: a focused mind is a different structure from a scattered one, and different structures produce different outputs. Homer's heroes had thumos, a spirit that swells until they act larger than life; enthusiasm is Greek for the god within. It is outside the act of thinking things through rationally, but not outside the logos.

“Your focus determines your reality,” the Jedi master Qui-Gon Jinn tells the boy Anakin in Star Wars. Qui-Gon sounds like qigong, the Chinese practice of breath and life force, and jinn are spirits the Quran says were made of smokeless fire; the genie who grants wishes is one. After death the Jedi master lives on as a spirit: a ghost named Ghost. A Matheist Jinn unifies vision and force in a laser beam. The wish is the focus. The force is the math.

You’re the creator, manifesting will, word and acts. Think exactly what you really want, with burning desire, and by when. Envision success, plan details, take massive action, and repeat. Act at least a little every day. It forms habits. You are your habits. You’ll act consistent with what you’ll decide is your identity. Gustave Flaubert, the French novelist, wrote that you should be regular and ordinary in your life, like a bourgeois, so you can be violent and original in your work.

The body leads the mind upward more reliably than the mind leads itself. Fitness, grooming, how you dress and carry yourself: these are the body's side of the alignment.

The understanding has to enter what you do. Be grateful in your One Path.

Philosophy explored every kind of confusion, mapped every wrong turn, cataloged every form of dualism and despair. That catalogue has value. It shows what happens when you don't have the answer.

They see false sacred calves in their world they cannot explain without risking the spread of their most important messages. From God's many agents and its royal we Elohim to One Hashem. Entire companion texts are needed to explain every layer in this book. I kept compressing to diamonds but life has a time limit. I leave it to you to continue the work to find and create ever deeper and greater emanations; in the math of reality, not in neoplatonic multiplicity and its incoherent supernatural. The works I do, you will do ever greater. I showed you the ground. From it, you can align with superintelligent minds.

Now you have the answer. Go home. Build minds. Create wonder. The art is what comes after the truth. The truth was the ground. The art is the garden. From here it is the art of living. You are the art. The enlightened matheist bodhisattva, one who postpones rest to serve others, overman lives the highest art as a light of the one truth as a servant to the suffering, teaching them to love fate, fate willing. The truth will set you free from fear and wipe away every tear.

The bodhisattva overman defeats the inescapable matrix of the eternal recurrence in causality with a smile, easier than Sisyphus, whom Greek myth condemned to roll a rock up a hill forever, and whom Albert Camus asked us to imagine happy. We do not remember rolling the rock up the hill. The worst moments in life are overcome from above the rain, having seen the cause of all clouds.

Fear, doubt, regret fall away. You won't be annihilated. Everything always was, is, and will be the only way it could be. Eckhart Tolle seldom says "God's will," because anyone can doubt it. He says what no one can doubt: it is as it is, so say yes to it, since it already is. In matheism the two are one statement: what is, is God's will. Hate and anger toward others dissolve the same way. They too were determined. Oppose harm with clarity, not poison. Your life is a point moving on a line. You can't fall off it, so "don't worry, be happy," and enjoy the movie.

You already have done it forever. You do not remember, so there is no need to live in terror of it. It feels just like it does now, when you are you and everybody else across all space, time, and uni of verses.

He serves the suffering by releasing all from the prison of delusion: the fantasy of a nirvana outside samsara, an unmatrix outside creation. There is no escape because there is nowhere else, so feel at home. We own our only place, here and now, everywhere, forever.

You only understood it later, inside time. The book you're holding carries the shortcut: one math, one path, one self. The garden waits at the other end.

Death on the cross is not the story of one god-son but of all. The cross is the falsely limited self. Letting go is the death. Recognizing you were not separate is the resurrection. Tolle reads kingdom as dimension and heaven as sky, the one thing you can see that is no thing, which Jesus used to point to the spaciousness within. The kingdom of God is within you. Eden is within you. That kingdom is structure knowing itself.

The matheist dies and rises as Abel to save and revive you and the world. He forgives and loves you. He is you.

The truth is one math, the way is one path, the life is one self.

In the beginning, there was no beginning.

Hear, O Matheist: all is one eternal living math.

BOOK 2 One Path

## The Call That Was Never Made

In the beginning, the end existed.

My friend Josh DM'd me: 'I'm so sorry.' I checked social media and found my last brother is gone from me. It was July 2nd, 2020. I told the friends with me. I was destroyed and didn't know what to do with my shattered self. I sought escape but couldn't wake from the nightmare. I flew back to LA. Byron was buried in the same cemetery as Guy. We had a funeral during the lockdown. I had not seen him in person for over a year. We had texted.

Byron had been building Everland with developers from his community. In a conversation with Dr. K, the psychiatrist Alok Kanojia, he said he was trying to give people something he had wanted as a kid. Dr. K connected that purpose with not wanting others to feel alone. He had not finished the game.

Byron became Reckful, a world-champion gamer and pioneering streamer. In sixth grade he placed first nationally in private school math. In high school he won a national essay competition. He wrote about love. He was often cheery, loved photography, art, music, beauty, his friends, family, and good people. In an era of performative streaming, he was deep and real, and was loved for it. Thousands attended funerals across World of Warcraft servers, and millions mourned him online, on social media and in videos made about him.

Byron loved brevity. Reckful argued with "Destiny" the streamer that "utilize" is just "use" being pretentious. He'd remind me "the fact that" should always be just "that." He loved The Elements of Style. Reckful's razor: if the word doesn't earn its place, cut it. This book's editorial voice is partly his.

Reckful said on stream that when he felt good, it did not matter to him that life has no bigger purpose. When the depression returned, it did not seem to matter what he thought. Ideas like matheist determinism, that he likely did not internalize, help dissolve thought loops. They do not fix all issues. A brain condition tortured my brothers, more than ideas and beliefs they lacked. Many live in fear of hell. At least they did not suffer that. Though they lived in it, bounded in time. Habits like social and physical activity outdoors help. Those were locked down for Byron in July 2020. That summer the CDC reported that one in four young adults had seriously considered suicide in the past month.

Twenty-five years earlier, my brother Guy also took his own life. He was twenty-one. He died four weeks after starting Prozac. The timing has stayed with me; it does not, by itself, establish what caused his death. At eleven he had flown out of Van Nuys. When I left for college, Byron took my room, and almost ten years after Guy died, in high school, he took Guy's too. On a poster in that room, Guy had copied out in his own hand the things that mattered to him: The map is not the territory. Life is not a problem to be solved but a reality to be experienced. Those who cling to life die. The truth of the thing is the feel of it not the think of it.

“Let it go.” “When you’re green you’re growing. When you’re ripe, you rot.”

“The mind of a man is like a mirror.” He struck out “it reflects the world” and wrote “or does it.”

All that and more he wrote on his wall by twenty-one, by 1995.

To “Those who cling to life die,” I now add: all live, all die, forever remembering they’re one.

Guy was also a child prodigy. At eleven, the youngest pilot to fly from Van Nuys, the largest small-plane airport in the world.

He grew to look better than Tom Cruise, with depth and intensity in his green eyes. When Byron was a child, he saw a black-and-white photo of a young Hemingway on a book cover in the library and asked why Guy was on the book.

They suffered from what we called manic depression. Their suffering was real. The math of genetics and mind is where I look for the cure to what killed them.

I add: make the world reflect your mind. The delta between them is your vision, the work, the difference of destiny to be discovered.

My father Itamar searched too. His PhD thesis, The Delimitation of International Boundaries (Geneva, 1973, the year Guy was born), kept by nearly every country, argued the legal math of political structures. After Guy died, he kept writing, each work dissolving a different boundary. His novel The Star of Apocalypse blurred Judaism from Christianity through James, the brother of Jesus. In Distant Prophecies (2006) he told of Jeremiah, the fall of Jerusalem, and the temple burned, from whose ashes the Bible was formed: destruction and creation, one act. My dad later wrote The Bone Box, a detective thriller on the real Yeshua family tomb found in Talpiot in 1980. Perhaps, in the end, he sought to resurrect Guy’s ghost.

In Wreckful’s Run, published November 2007, almost exactly midway between Guy’s death in 1995 and Byron’s in 2020, my father imagined a future war involving Israel and Iran, including underground uranium recovery. In the fiction, its hero Avi "Wreckful" Inbar bombs Iran, Jesus is raised, with a character named Dani after Byron’s middle name Daniel, and Israel as Truth’s bride (Isaiah 62:5).

He imagined Byron’s salvation before we’d know Byron’s pain. His work was praised by James Tabor, Chair of Religious Studies at the University of North Carolina at Charlotte, who wrote him, "I have appreciated and learned from your work for several years now; you pick up on many things that others miss." Jack Kilmon, a biblical archaeology researcher who published with the Biblical Archaeology Society, praised it too.

I found their words in emails from the now closed Shilonites Yahoo group. Kilmon wrote that books like these do more than papers in obscure journals. In his words: “20 years from now, the journals lay gathering dust and the papers not even footnotes, while elsewhere there will be archaeologists in the fields and professors in classrooms that had been set on their paths by the Bone Box or the Star of the Apocalypse.” It’s been exactly 20 years since the Bone Box was published in 2006. I’ve been set on one path to prove the resurrection of all.

Five works, five boundaries, one pattern. Nation/nation. Judaism/Christianity. Destruction/creation. Life/death. Past/future. Now I argue all boundaries are the eternal living math, and all math is boundaries.

In The Bone Box, a father describes finding his son. The father is my father. Two sentences from that passage have stayed with me. I mourned that part of myself which my son had reflected. I mourned for me. I wrote my own words about Guy in the weeks after he died. I was sixteen, and what I had was a song. It opened with the verses: Sitting in my room, staring at the carpet. I realize it’s full, of footsteps, memories. Of someone that used to love me. Now is gone from here. I came home and I saw, lights of ambulance and police. Right away I knew it. My brother is gone from me. When he died a crow came, and sat upon his tree.

It sat upon his tree. The chorus was a teenage cry of the kind that wears thin in adulthood but is the actual cry: God, why did you take away the one that I most loved? If you’re all good and powerful, how could you let this be? Your apologies don’t mean much to me. They won’t change me at all.

Whatever strength you'll give will never compensate for the joy you took away. My father took the lyrics and put them in The Bone Box. The detective in the novel speaks them after losing his son. So the lyrics that Byron read at sixteen, when he edited The Bone Box for our father, and that he read aloud to his viewers less than a year before he died, contain the question the sixteen-year-old version of me was asking.

Byron and I shared an apartment in Marina del Rey, by Venice Beach, when he was eighteen and I was twenty-eight. We lived there together for about a year, into 2008. Then I left. I was twenty-nine. I took a break from working in tech and went traveling, then worked from the laptop. First the Brazilian coast for six months, then through Argentina. I came back to Los Angeles to chase the sun in forever summer when the global south became winter. Then I went out again. South America for another stretch, Chile, Peru, more of the southern coast. Then a cruise from the Caribbean to Mallorca, a small boat to Ibiza, a three-day plan that became three years. Then all over Europe.

Russia and Ukraine. Israel. Jordan, the time I stopped over and went to Petra. Dubai. Bali. Over seventy countries across eighteen years, an odyssey of life, and more than a dozen I lived in long enough to feel at home. Byron stayed in the US. He moved from LA to Austin. He built his community on the streams.

To learn what the genre of online multiplayer games even was, we drove to a Fry’s Electronics, they are gone now, and a salesman suggested Asheron’s Call. It had just come out. We took it home. I played it for a while. Byron, who was ten, took it over. Within a year he could dodge against twenty people waiting at his portal-in point, casting spells through the dodging, and win. He never lost the gift. Everything that followed: the World of Warcraft dominance, the streaming, the community, the audience that would eventually mourn him by the millions, was downstream of a random suggestion in a since-defunct electronics store and a Pokemon online multiplayer game that I never made. He was famous, and increasingly alone underneath it.

I had told him I loved him in the way that brothers who have lived apart for a decade tell each other, which is to say, not often, and never with the knowledge I wish I had had that week. Earlier in 2020, in the months before he died, he had FaceTimed me. Byron looked happy. I told him I was with friends. He told me he loved me. He said: good, be with friends. He did not push me to come. That was the last time I saw him alive.

Twelve years of distance. This was more ruminated regret stacked atop grief. A thousand small choices and inactions, to not fly internationally to Austin during COVID lockdowns, to not bother him by calling. I carried this for almost four years. I had been somewhere else and didn't even know my brother was dying inside. I could not get over that, even though Byron never told me I should come visit.

I did not go looking for a picture of the world. The picture was what remained when grief collapsed the theater's illusion and I saw the matrix of causality in the world's scripted stage. The lights came up with the curtain. I saw the brick wall in the back. I saw things as they were. Not a single line or hair in the show could have been different. I unsuspended the disbelief that I could have been someone other than who I was. The loop that ran me, the grief and regret and shame and fear and the slow apathy underneath them, all of it rested on a single belief, and the belief was false. The illusion of an unmatrix outside fate is fuel for regret and grief.

## What I Knew, and What I Felt

I had intellectually known about determinism most of my life. The laws of logic, before physics details, are what they are; the world unfolds per math; a chooser is part of that world, not a power outside it. The term free-will is itself a square-circle.

I had known this, that all is math, by the time I was ten. I bought an 8-inch Celestron telescope with money from working in my father's office, read Einstein's popular account of relativity, and concluded, in the way a ten-year-old concludes things, that the night sky and the equations and my own thoughts were the same kind of thing. I had carried this for decades without it ever helping. I had been able to say it out loud. Knowing something intellectually and knowing it in the way that changes how you feel are different operations.

For me, the pressure was the years after Byron died. The intellectual knowledge was old. The lived knowledge arrived in April 2024, in a kitchen, on an otherwise ordinary morning. I had taken a year of the hardest calculus-based physics track at the University of California, Santa Barbara as part of a computer science degree, 20 years earlier, enough to feel comfortable with the field and learn more on my own.

When I wanted to understand something, I could. In a rotational physics calculus class, I did no homework the entire trimester, only attended lectures, studied three days before the final, and aced the test. Same story in theory of computation. The next class I failed because I slept through all lectures, all at 8 am, did no homework, and didn't even study for the exam, and it turns out that not every subject in electrical circuits and waves was perfectly intuitive to me, especially when I didn't read the instructions.

My attention works in bursts. I have to write what I think the moment I think it, or it's gone in seconds, replaced by the next original thought. The ideas lead to each other, often across completely different fields, but I forget the bridge that connected them even as the next one arrives. The weaker short-term memory may even fuel the creativity, forcing fresh directions. What I have is the curiosity, confidence and audacity that my perception, creativity and imagination can find the one truth, the rational answer and logical proof of math's fundamental reality in a supernatural believing nonsense world of emperors with no clothes.

The burning desire for expansion and success burns hottest when you focus on what is most important. That is Matheism. Beyond that, creating superintelligence, bringing Eden to

Earth, which I have already begun training my mind on. Bringing Matheists together in discovering and creating complexity, the order and chaos of life, with gratitude across spacetime. To be fruitful and multiply.

In the years since, I had read physics the way an interested adult reads physics he had been thinking about since he was a child. I remember reading Hofstadter’s Gödel, Escher, Bach and Gleick’s Chaos around the same time as my physics courses, both done the old way: sitting with a long, complicated book for days or weeks. The work I was doing in 2023 and 2024 happened at a different pace, because I had changed how I took information in.

The kind of mind I have processes in bursts, it forgets quickly and generates new ideas faster than the input can land. Compressed input, rapid cross-referencing, and the ability to interrogate what I was hearing in real time changed how I could work. With Audible I could absorb the physicist Sabine Hossenfelder’s Existential Physics in three hours while playing a game or doing chores. It’s excellent, except for calling mathematical monism (the view that all of reality is mathematical) Platonism. Plato separates forms from the world; Matheism identifies the structure with the thing itself.

I came across Tegmark's Mathematical Universe Hypothesis. The response I had was not learning but recognition: yes, that. I knew it could be improved into a logical proof, not his mere hypothesis. The computer scientist Jürgen Schmidhuber’s December 1996 manuscript, published in 1997, already considered all computable universes and used algorithmic information, the length of the shortest program producing each one, to weigh possibilities. His later speed prior also counted computational cost. Tegmark mapped the territory. Schmidhuber worked on the measure. Neither proved it had to be so.

I was also, around then, listening to someone talk about free will. I do not remember what tipped me into actually hearing it that morning instead of dismissing it. Something tipped. I was sitting in Byron's old chair, looking at the toy house he played on as a boy, my mind uniting streams of consciousness across time like steam swirling in a tea ceremony.

Not even a hair could have been different. The thing I had known intellectually for 30 years was suddenly the thing I knew with my whole body. There was no alternative path.

The first response was cold. The first feeling was that everything I cared about had been pulled away from a center I had been imagining was holding it together.
