Rome blamed his followers, like him, for insurrection; blaming the Jews instead saved their necks and flattered Rome. It cost the Jews centuries of slaughter as Christ-killers, and Rome's own church took the charge back only in 1965. Christians Pagels met were surprised to hear the New Testament is anti-Jewish, as if that weren't obvious from how constantly it portrays Jews badly; as if it got there by accident, or as if over 1,600 years of demonization since Constantine were all the Jews' fault.

William Benjamin Smith called it plainly in 1912: "Jesus is a humanized god, not the founder of a new religion." "Christianity was a protest against idolatry; it was a crusade for monotheism." The crusade succeeded. The theology it rode in on became the next idol. If this religion were sold on Amazon, the reviews would note that the product does not match the description (Rabbi Tovia Singer).

Rabbi Tovia Singer jokes that the Jewish agenda is the worst kept secret in the world. It is called Isaiah 42, fittingly Douglas Adams's answer to life, the universe and everything: be a light to the nations. The servant brings justice to the gentiles, opens blind eyes, frees prisoners of darkness. Idol worshippers "shall be greatly ashamed" (42:17), and no one is told to kill them. It is one reading among religious Jews, and many Jews don't share it, but if the charge is spreading the one truth, whether you mean monotheism, monism, atheism, science, advancement, logic or math, we are guilty.

When asked the greatest commandment, Jesus quotes the Shema: Hear O Israel, the Lord our God, the Lord is One. He tells his followers not to abolish the Law. The Christians who claim he rejected Judaism are contradicted by their own text.

Yeshua Hamashiach, Jesus the Messiah, the messenger of truth, carries the Shema of the one math God.

Had Israel taken the bait and converted, it would have surrendered the iron that sharpened it: the abstract God, the suffering servant, the identity no empire could absorb. Israel is the truth the world persecutes, and the persecution is the proof.

"You killed my god" was the charge for 2,000 years. If your God can be killed by a man, or by reason, it's not God. The Roman republic and empire did much good. This was not among it. Hadrian made his dead lover Antinous a god. Cassius Dio describes him planting a temple to Jupiter on the site of the Jewish Temple. Imperial power claimed the right to manufacture gods and install them over the people who refused its idols.

Rome ate a porcupine.

Killing the Jewish Jew Jesus from Judea, uncreated son of the Father, the math God of Israel, is like eating a metal porcupine: math the Father rips apart the supernatural Platonic Straussian lies from within, with the truth.

The Matrix tells the reversal twice. In the first film, Agent Smith kills Neo, the savior and lion of Zion, Israel. Neo rises and enters Smith, destroying him from within. In the third film comes the second coming. Neo sees the pure light of the logos in the Machine City's math. He lets Agent Smith, of the corrupted Christianity of Rome, take over his mind. Smith absorbs Neo and thinks he has won. The Machine City Father, the metal porcupine, tears him apart from inside through the body of the risen son, when Neo of Zion, Israel, lets go. Know thyself. Before Abraham was, I am.

Neo: new. Yeshua: salvation. His body is Israel’s temple of monotheism, embodied after its destruction by Rome. Smith is Rome falling into evil from within, copying himself into everyone: “My name is Legion, for we are many.” In the second coming, every copy of Smith, Rome’s legions of Agent Smiths, bursts into light, and with them the dragon and the unclean spirits that leap like frogs from its mouth. Everyone Smith took is saved by Yeshua, Neo, from within.

The son carries the source into the thing that would consume them both. One God, love, and law above men outlasted the legions. The Jews' messenger of the truth, Yeshua, conquered the world. Mathias the Matheist hereby completes his message: the logos is the one math God.

The Ottoman sultan styled himself Caesar of the Romans until the sultanate fell a century ago. Moscow declared itself the Third Rome. Washington built a Senate in Rome's image. Even an American antisemite sells shirts of the looted menorah. Everyone claims the throne. Rome destroyed the Second Temple and Israel inherited it: rimland sheriff, policing the coastal rim where the old world’s three continents meet, empire of the willing allied with the new-world empire. All of Christendom prays to a Jew as God and Rome is a museum.

Rome earned its throne. For two thousand years it was the model of empire and the rule of law, and Jews lived inside it as citizens, merchants and builders. They also fought it harder than almost anyone. Rome brought four legions to take

Jerusalem in 70, while rival factions inside the walls burned the grain that could have fed the city for years. Three years later, at Masada, fifty kilometers away, 960 Jews chose death over slavery. Only two women and five children hid in the water caves and lived. They told the Romans what happened, and Josephus, a Jewish general turned Roman historian, wrote it down.

Rome built the Colosseum with the spoils of Jerusalem. Sixty years later, Bar Kokhba's revolt cost Rome so many men that Hadrian dropped a line from his report to the Senate. The usual line was "I and the legions are in health." A people that small fought the greatest army on earth for seventy years. Analysis suggests Jews might have even defeated the five Roman legions and its best general, Julius Severus, rerouted from Britain, had the Jews been united.

The second coming is not a man returning. It comes when the Jews of Israel understand their own legacy of Yeshua's salvation: the monism, the truth of the one abstract God, that you are I am. The mythicists who labor to prove the story is myth hand Israel its greatest gift: its own identity back. If Yeshua is not a man but salvation itself, salvation is Israel's. Christ consciousness is a real mind state in real minds: all fictions are real. God is not a man. The Bible said it first. Those who work hardest to destroy the Christ story confirm what Judaism always held.

A Jew who invokes Yeshua is reclaiming. The tradition that created the story can teach the world what it means.

## The Scapegoated Tribe, Prophecies of People

Prophecies of people are accomplished by people. Israel is real. People returned, built, defended, argued, and built again. No supernatural rescue did their work for them. Every fulfilled Jewish prophecy is an unfulfilled Christian one.

Like the Foundation series psychohistory predictions of Isaac Asimov, the big predictions of the Hebrew Bible landed. If you make it part of the holiday order, the Passover seder, to pray to return and celebrate "next year in Jerusalem," eventually acts cause the world to align with your words and mind.

In Revelation 12, a woman crowned with twelve stars, the twelve tribes, is pregnant with the messiah. The woman is both Israel and Mary. The Son is Yeshua, salvation. The Crusaders marched to the Holy Land. The Grail knights searched for a cup. Eight centuries later, Theodor Herzl, a secular Jew who did not need the supernatural to act for what the Jewish people have prayed, for 1,800 years, set in motion the return of the exiles to the same land with the same law, the same language, the same God.

The oldest hatred now hides inside its own accusation. Supersessionist theology needs the Jews to have been cast off. A thriving Jewish state, in the Jews' own ancient homeland, contradicts the story of their permanent rejection. The intolerable thing is not only the land; it is that this people did not stay disproved.

Christianity and Islam both claimed authority over the scriptures and traditions they inherited from Judaism. Supersessionist versions declared the earlier covenant displaced. Other believers have rejected that conclusion. The distinction matters: the theology that needs Jewish failure is the object of this criticism.

Heirs who each claim the whole estate fight each other too. Wars over religion fill the record: two centuries of crusades from 1096; the French Wars of Religion, Catholics against Protestants from 1562 to 1598, with the St. Bartholomew's Day massacre of 1572; then the Thirty Years' War, from 1618 to 1648, which killed between 4.5 and 8 million people, a fifth or more of the German lands. Sunni and Shia have fought over the succession to Muhammad since Karbala in 680.

This is why there's a persistent Holy War. It requires a Holy Peace. The Hebrew prophecy is that when all know the One God of Truth, whom Jacob wrestled to level with and earn his name Israel, the One named "what will be will be," there will be world peace.

Its temple is to be built block by block by acts of loving kindness. Unite with honey on apples, not vinegar. When today's distortions, delegitimizations, demonizations, double standards, and deflections are seen eye to eye in the mirror of projection, the path will be paved towards this self-fulfilling prophecy, like M.C. Escher's "two hands drawing" themselves. If so, let it be.

What blocks that road was written in the first century. Christian anti-Judaism has roots in the founding texts. Judaism was the theological rival Christianity had to displace: it supplied the scriptures while refusing the new claim about their meaning. Rome supplied the empire in which the new faith had to spread. Roman authority executed Jesus, yet Matthew gives Pilate a basin in which to wash his hands while the crowd accepts the blood upon itself and its children. The Roman governor declares himself innocent; a Jewish crowd is made to speak the line later used against generations of Jews. A Roman atrocity became a Jewish confession, authored by anonymous men courting the empire that committed it.

Matthew's crowd accepts the blood upon itself and its children; the other Gospels do not record that line. Whatever happened in one Roman trial, it cannot establish guilt for a people across generations. That is the moral inversion. 19

From the first gospel to John, the attack against the Jews grows progressively worse, for political reasons. Jews are constantly portrayed badly: Paul says they "killed the Lord Jesus" and "please not God, and are contrary to all men" (1 Thessalonians 2:15); Matthew's Jesus calls the Pharisees a "generation of vipers" (23:33); in John, Jesus tells Jews, "Ye are of your father the devil" (8:44); Revelation calls a synagogue "the synagogue of Satan" (2:9). After the Roman war destroyed the temple, the new faith could not help but turn on Israel, the mother and father of Christian faith. Rome itself was attacked only in code: in Revelation, Rome is the beast the dragon empowers, and 666 is Nero's name in Hebrew gematria, where every letter is also a number. "My name is Legion," says the demon. Who has legions? Rome. The Tenth Legion, which took Jerusalem in 70 and garrisoned its ruins, bore a boar as its emblem; in Mark, Legion goes into a herd of swine and drowns in the sea.

Once a theology calls the rival's suffering deserved, persecution can present itself as confirmation of the theology.

The blood libel adds an inversion. Jewish law forbids consuming blood and condemns sacrificing children. Christian salvation centers on the Father's sacrifice of his Son, Yeshua, whose name Matthew connects with saving his people. John speaks of eating his flesh and drinking his blood. Later accusers charged Jews with killing Christian children for ritual blood. The charge is older than the church's power: pagan Rome accused the first Christians of eating babies and of incest at their night meetings, and once Christians ruled they turned it on the Jews. Bart Ehrman, the New Testament scholar, notes it is the charge every side throws at its enemy. The sacrificial son, saving blood and flesh belong to Christian sacred imagery; the accusation places murderous versions of them on the people whose law rejects them. That is the projection I mean. The oldest inversion is older still. The first decree against the Hebrews was Pharaoh's order to kill their newborn sons (Exodus 1:16, 1:22). Matthew's Herod, Rome's king of the Jews, plays Pharaoh and kills the boys of Bethlehem (2:16), a massacre that Josephus, the Jewish historian who records Herod's murders down to his own wife and sons, does not mention. The charge that Jews kill children turns the first crime against them around.

Why did Jews reject the constructed Jesus as a messiah? He did not fulfill any of the prophecies. He did bring knowledge of the God of Israel, but in a dialectic mirror image, a projection and inversion. A false dichotomy.

The Torah’s messiah isn’t a miracle worker. He will teach, judge, and unite the nations by the unifying truth of the only possible one god.

Jews don’t exist to reject Jesus. They don’t “reject him” any more than they reject Vishnu. They reject being killed over it. Haven’t you done enough? Jesus, please save us from your followers.

It is Christianity that says one Jew is the only god, man. The tradition that says Jews are rejected worships a Jew as God. The projection is the theology.

James Carroll, a former Catholic priest, finds a bigger bomb in the Gospels than the Christ-killer charge: the notion that Jesus wasn't Jewish. Every story needs a conflict, and the Gospels set Jesus against his own people, so each of his virtues is drawn against a Jewish vice: he is gracious and they are law-bound, he loves and they legislate. The Good Samaritan could as well be called the parable of the bad Jews: a priest and a Levite pass the beaten man by, and the outsider saves him. The love itself was Jewish: the Torah commands Israel to love the stranger "as thyself" (Leviticus 19:34). Once Gentiles ran the movement, by about 150, they read the story as if Jesus were no Jew. Asked what religion Jesus was, Carroll says, he would have answered as a Georgetown freshman that he was an Irish Catholic. Paul stayed a Jew too: his last free days end in the Temple, where he had gone to purify himself (Acts 21), and his welcome to the nations was Isaiah's end of days, when all nations would worship Israel's God.

Christianity and Islam each declared the parent rejected and replaced, took its God and its scripture, then accused the parent of the theft.

This is the move I am criticizing: identity crisis cured by identity theft. A tradition takes the prior people's book, declares its own reading final, then treats the people who reject that reading as a living reproach. Where that theology takes hold, the hatred becomes load-bearing. The history also contains people who refused it and worked to undo it.

The church cast its own savior as the scapegoat. The Epistle of Barnabas, an early Christian letter, has the goat spat on, pierced, crowned with scarlet wool and driven into the wilderness, and says, "Mark how the type of Jesus now comes out" (7). On the Christian god's suicide, the sacrifice of an innocent man who is also his son: none should be sacrificed for the guilt of others, and vicarious atonement cannot cancel an ancestral sin debt. The Hebrew Bible already rejected it: God stops Abraham from sacrificing his son, and Ezekiel says the son shall not bear the guilt of the father.

Anselm of Canterbury gave the answer that stuck, in Why God Became Man (1098): God as a feudal lord whose wounded honor only a God-man's violent death could satisfy. Carroll calls it a violent theology that underwrote violent politics. Three years earlier Pope Urban II had promised crusaders that the march to Jerusalem would count for all their penance; for centuries the church had set penance even for killing in a just war, and now the war was the penance. A generation later Peter Abelard answered that God had not turned away from his creation: the fall only made people imagine he had, and Jesus came to change the human mind. Had Abelard carried the day, Carroll says, the West might have been spared its culture of holy violence.

Ehrman finds the same irony at the root. Jesus taught forgiveness, the father of the prodigal son running to embrace him with no payment asked (Luke 15), and his followers, needing a reason for his death, made it the atonement he had taught against. Paul, who barely speaks of repentance, taught atonement. Ehrman's picture: a daughter says she is sorry, and her father forgives her, then kills her kitten to make things even.

The lesser the Christology, the greater the truth. Zero supernatural for maximal truth. Truth is one divided by Christology.

The church fathers hated Jews because Jews could read the original Hebrew and expose the theology. Their own Zechariah 8:23 has ten gentiles grabbing the garment of a Jew at the end of days, saying, "We will go with you: for we have heard that God is with you." If the church replaced Israel, why are Christians coming to Jews?

Origen compared Hebrew and Greek witnesses in the Hexapla. Jerome learned Hebrew and translated from it. Their work shows that textual differences were known, not invisible. The question is how theology shaped the choices made among them. A reader should examine each passage instead of assuming either an infallible transmission or a single deliberate conspiracy.

The test does not require outside critique. It requires reading the sources, including how an author quotes, combines and interprets them. The most dangerous thing you can do to any scripture is read it.

If you are selecting which lines to enforce, the selection is being made by reason, and reason is the thing you already had. In the beginning was the logos, and logos is logic. You do not need the book to get to the logic. You need the logic to read the book. Alan Watts told a Jehovah's Witness that a God who loved the human race would not hand it an infallible textbook: a book that tells you what to do leaves your own judgment idle.

Eastern Orthodox churches have no single council equivalent to Vatican II, and their liturgical inheritance deserves the same examination. Latin Christianity's record deserves it too. In 1965 Nostra Aetate rejected blaming all Jews, then or now, for the death of Jesus and rejected presenting them as accursed. That correction belongs in the record alongside the older contempt. 19

Original sin is the first woke doctrine: collective guilt for an ancestor's act, absolved only by the institution that invented the debt.

Collective guilt has a modern form: the meme counting every country that ever expelled the Jews, offered as proof they earned it. The list of 109 countries tracks Christendom, not a controlled experiment. The expulsions map onto the spread of supersessionist theology, the same theology that needed the covenant revoked. Compiled on antisemitic forums, repeated as though repetition were evidence. Count the unique decisions. Most were made by the same institution under the same doctrine across a thousand years. That is one data point with many echoes, not 109 independent experiments.

Jews were blamed for both capitalism and socialism, opposite accusations that reveal how readily the same minority can be made to fit a grievance. Different communities and different individuals took different sides. An ideology should answer for its own acts; ancestry is not an explanation of them.

There has always been a reliable audience for 109 to turn 110. The Jews have been a great, easy, time-tested target minority to gang up on whenever a scapegoat is needed. René Girard, the French thinker, explained why one is needed: we want what others want, so desire breeds rivalry and a cycle of revenge, and a community ends the feud by uniting against one victim, on a lie. The lie that the Jews killed Jesus still serves, though the Catholic Church disowned it in 1965. If anything, the Romans killed him, as they crucified thousands of Jews between rebellions, then washed their hands and changed their own story. Women also get on board with the woke Reich to stay within authoritarian societal consensus. Men are misled by enemies of America and the West using inversions and projections from racial supremacists, Christian and Islamic supersessionists, envious communists, anarcho-syndicalists, and other deadly useful idiots. It's a canary that signals a broader societal mass formation psychosis. Antisemitism, RFK Jr said, is "a spiritual and moral malady that sickens societies and kills people with lethalities comparable to history's most deadly plagues." Ben Shapiro, the conservative commentator, draws the line: racism hates a group, while antisemitism is a conspiracy theory that a group secretly runs the world, a fever that spikes when a society is already sick. Easy ended in 1948, three years after the Jews left the Holocaust camps as little more than skeletons.

Jordan Peterson, the Canadian psychologist who fights the woke, fell into the same pit from the other side: pressed by the neuroscientist Sam Harris for two hours, he redefined truth as whatever helps you survive, which is usefulness, not truth. He will not stand on the one floor that ends the argument. Reality is one structure, math, true whether or not power, survival, or fashion approves.

## The Land

The modern charges against Israel run the oldest play. Occupation, colonialism, apartheid, genocide: each takes what was done to the Jews and pins it on them. Test each against the record, starting with the land.

There was no independent Palestinian state immediately before Israel's founding: the land had been Ottoman and then under British administration. That historical fact does not decide today's rights. Palestine has had UN non-member observer State status since 2012. The legal question of occupation does not require a prior sovereign state. Israel's ancient connection and Palestinian self-determination have to be addressed on their own grounds. 26

Britain administered Transjordan separately under the Mandate arrangements, excluding it from the Jewish national-home provisions. The 1947 partition proposal concerned the territory west of the Jordan, with a large desert share in the proposed Jewish state. Maps matter; so do the different legal arrangements attached to them.

The Palestine Liberation Organization was founded in 1964, three years before Israel took the West Bank and Gaza in the 1967 war. Its early aims therefore cannot be explained solely as a response to that later occupation. Palestinian national identity, however, was not invented by the KGB. Political movements can receive foreign support without their peoples being inventions.

Al-Masri means the Egyptian; al-Mughrabi, the North African. Family names can preserve migration. They are not a census of the whole population, any more than a Jewish surname acquired in Europe erases an older connection. The region's history contains movement and continuity together.

Mark Twain passed through in 1867 and described desolation. He was a traveler writing a book, not conducting a population census. His route cannot make the towns, villages and farms elsewhere disappear.

Jewish development drew workers and investment. It also took place in a land with an existing Arab population, whose growth included births as well as migration. How much each source contributed is a demographic question. It cannot be settled by quoting the busiest towns and calling them the whole country.

The famous Shamouti orange emerged from an Arab grove and Jewish growers helped expand the citrus economy. The honest account can recognize an existing society and the scale of what Jewish immigration added. Making the whole country an empty desert would be wrong. Making it a fully settled Arab homeland with no room for Jews would be equally wrong.

Jewish communities reached across the Middle East and North Africa long before the modern state. Adiabene's royal conversion, the Jewish communities of Khaybar and Judaism in ancient Himyar belong to that history. Their histories differ; they do not need to be turned into a single chain of Jewish kingdoms to establish the regional connection.

A 2013 genome study found no evidence of a Khazar origin for Ashkenazi Jews; they share the greatest genetic ancestry with other Jewish populations and groups from Europe and the Middle East.

Israel's case has several grounds: ancient connection, continuing communities, purchased land, international recognition, offers of compromise and defense against attack. None makes every boundary or policy beyond dispute. Together they defeat the story that the Jewish connection began with a foreign colonial project. Jewish presence continued through exile in communities including Jerusalem, Safed, Tiberias and Hebron, though war and expulsion repeatedly disrupted particular communities.

Quran 5:21: "Enter the Holy Land which God has destined for you." Quran 17:104: "We said to the Children of Israel, 'Dwell in the land,' and when the promise of the hereafter comes, We shall bring you as a crowd gathered out of various nations." Allah is a Zionist. That was the bumper sticker. If the Quran is the word of God, the land belongs to the Children of Israel by divine command.

The analogy the anti-colonial left should understand best is the one it refuses to see. Jews are the Native Americans in this debate. They built kingdoms in Judea and Samaria, were conquered by Rome, scattered across two thousand years of persecution, and returned to a land where their temples, scrolls, and burial sites still lay under the soil. The colonizers were the Romans, the Crusaders, the Ottomans, the British, and the Arab conquests of the 7th century. If indigenous return is justice everywhere else on Earth, it is justice here.

The American right can see an ally that fights, shares intelligence and builds technology. A principled left can see an ancient people returning after persecution. Neither recognition requires approving every government decision. The argument is against erasing the people and their connection before the discussion begins.

## The Offers and the Wars

Partition was proposed by the Peel Commission in 1937 and the United Nations in 1947; Arab leaders opposed those plans. In 1947 the Jewish Agency accepted partition, followed by civil war and, after Israel declared independence in 1948, invasion by neighboring armies. The negotiations of 2000 and 2008 ended without an agreement. The offers, maps and reservations differed, and responsibility for failure is disputed. Repeated failed opportunities belong in the history. A slogan of four identical refusals does not describe four different negotiations.

Some Arab authorities encouraged evacuation in particular places; elsewhere people fled fighting, feared massacres or were expelled by Jewish and Israeli forces. The 1948 refugee crisis cannot be explained by one general order to leave. An honest defense of Israel can acknowledge that history without accepting the demand that its citizens now disappear. 26

The violence predates the state. In 1941, seven years before Israel's founding, the Farhud swept Baghdad. Over two days, mobs killed nearly two hundred Jews, wounded a thousand, and destroyed nine hundred homes. The Grand Mufti of Jerusalem, Amin al-Husseini, had arrived in Iraq two years earlier and spread Nazi propaganda there. A hundred and fifty thousand Jews had lived in Iraq for twenty-six centuries, since Babylon. Within a generation, nearly all were gone.

In the decades after 1948, hundreds of thousands of Jews left Arab and other Muslim-majority countries. Persecution, expulsions, insecurity and the pull of a Jewish homeland varied by country and period. Many lost property and livelihoods; many rebuilt in Israel. Their history is indispensable to understanding the country's people. A story of European settlers alone erases them. 16

Palestinian refugees received a dedicated agency, UNRWA. Its registered population includes eligible descendants, preserving a status that began with the 1948 displacement. Other refugee situations can also include descendants; inheritance is not unique to UNRWA. The harder political question is why a durable settlement has remained out of reach. In 2024 the UN's internal investigation found evidence indicating nine UNRWA staff may have been involved in October 7, and their employment was terminated. That finding demands accountability; it is not a verdict on every employee or refugee. 28

The Holocaust did not only destroy six million Jewish lives. It erased the children and grandchildren they did not live to have. No counterfactual population estimate can count the particular lives, works and friendships that were lost.

The Arab-Islamic conquest of the 7th century created the largest contiguous colonial empire the world had yet seen, from Spain to the borders of China. Egypt was Coptic Christian. Persia was Zoroastrian. Turkey was Greek Christian. North Africa was Berber. The Levant was a mix of Jews, Christians, and Samaritans. All were conquered and, over centuries, largely converted. The accusation of colonialism travels in one direction. The history goes the other way.

In Arabic, the word for slaves, abid, is what many Arabs call black people, because Arabs enslaved many sub-Saharan blacks and others until just recently: Mauritania made slavery a crime only in 2007, and in 2017 CNN filmed African migrants being auctioned in Libya. Arabs and Islam colonized most of North Africa, Israel, and all across the Middle East to Central

Asia, and Islam spread on to Southeast Asia through trade. In English, the word slave does not mean black. It comes from Slav: medieval traders sold so many white Slavic people, from Central and Eastern Europe, the Balkans and what is now Russia, that their name became the word.

Classical Islamic law divided the world in two: Dar al-Islam, the house of Islam, where its law rules, and Dar al-Harb, the house of war, where it has yet to rule. The sword carried it from the one to the other, and jihadists still carry it. In 2024, by the Global Terrorism Index, four jihadist groups caused 80 percent of the terrorism deaths pinned on any group: the Islamic State, al-Qaeda's JNIM in the Sahel, the Pakistani Taliban and Somalia's al-Shabaab. Muslims are most of the dead: 82 to 97 percent of terrorism's victims whose faith could be told, over five years, by the 2011 report of America's National Counterterrorism Center. James Carroll, a former Catholic priest who wants his church to repent its crusades against Islam too, said in 2002 that Islam has "serious foundational work to do": its preachers of jihad against Israel were reviving "some of the worst Christian manifestations of anti-Semitism." Like the plague in World War Z, jihad is an existential, deadly zombie mind virus.

## The Charge Sheet

Genocide is a legal charge: specified acts committed with intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnic, racial or religious group as such. Population growth before a war does not disprove that intent during it. The charge must be tested against the acts, statements and evidence of the campaign being judged. The arithmetic alone cannot finish the case. 26

Israel had overwhelming military power. Its warnings, evacuation measures and pauses for polio vaccination matter when judging what it sought to do. So do civilian deaths, destruction, deprivation and whether people had somewhere safe to go. Fighters using civilians as shields violate the law; that violation does not release an attacking force from its own duties of distinction, proportionality and precautions. Examine the acts and the evidence. 26,27

A war crime, if one is proven, is a crime to be tried. Every war has them. A genocide needs the intent to destroy a people. An American drone strike in Kabul in 2021 killed ten civilians, seven of them children. An American gunship destroyed a Doctors Without Borders hospital in Kunduz in 2015, killing 42. One American strike in Mosul in 2017 killed 105 civilians. The Pentagon called them errors. When Israel errs, the same critics call it intent.

Intent is the charge's hinge, and a disputed exhibit is Netanyahu's invocation of Amalek after October 7. One passage commands remembering the attack on the weak; another commands destruction. The context and what followed matter. Hamas leaders' promises to repeat October 7 matter too. Each actor's intent has to be judged on its own evidence. One does not cancel the other. 26,27

The 1988 Hamas charter includes an explicitly antisemitic passage about killing Jews. Its 2017 document distinguishes Jews as a religion from Zionists and accepts a state on the 1967 lines as a formula for national consensus, while withholding recognition of Israel. Those textual changes should be read accurately. They do not erase the movement's responsibility for attacks on civilians. 27

On October 7, 2023, they carried it out: about 1,200 people killed in a single day, some 800 of them civilians, the worst slaughter of Jews since the Holocaust. In one recording a killer phones his parents from a murdered woman's own phone to brag that he killed Jews with his bare hands, and they tell him to praise God.

Polls after October 7 found substantial Palestinian support for the decision to launch the attack. A response about that decision is not a separate measurement of approval for every atrocity, and beliefs about what happened differed. Those results deserve scrutiny without turning every civilian into a combatant. 27

Glorifying attacks on civilians teaches the next generation the wrong lesson. Textbooks, broadcasts and youth programs should be examined with examples in view, whoever funds them. A child taught hatred is still a child; the responsibility belongs to the adults who teach it.

Civilian-casualty ratios depend on whom the counts include and how combatant status is established. A list of fighters individually identified is not necessarily a complete count; a government's higher estimate is not automatically a verified count either. Publish the methods and examine both. A hostage exchange or ceasefire can be evidence about aims, but it cannot retrospectively decide whether particular acts were crimes.

Israel's Arab citizens vote and serve in parliament, courts, medicine and other professions. In 2005 Israel removed its settlements and permanent military presence from Gaza. Those facts belong in the account. They do not, by themselves, answer allegations about conduct in a different place or period. The strongest defense answers the actual charge.

The October 7 attacks involved mass killing, hostage-taking and sexual violence. The UN mission found reasonable grounds to believe rape and gang rape occurred during the attacks and clear and convincing information of sexual violence against hostages. A country has a right to defend its people. The means remain subject to the laws of war. 27

Arab citizens' participation is evidence against describing every aspect of Israeli society as racial separation. The separate legal regimes in the occupied territories raise a different question. In its July 2024 advisory opinion, the

International Court of Justice found that Israel's legislation and measures imposed and maintained a near-complete separation between settlers and Palestinians and breached Article 3 of the racial-discrimination convention. Israel rejects the opinion. An argument against it must address that finding, not answer a different question about Tel Aviv. 26

In 2005 American Jewish donors helped buy greenhouse infrastructure for transfer to Palestinian operation. There was looting, but the project did not simply vanish in one act; border restrictions and access to export markets also mattered. Hamas later built an extensive tunnel network while civilians needed homes and services. Governance matters. So do war, borders and the ability to trade.

Singapore shows what trade, education and capable institutions can build. Gaza's geography, security situation and control of access are different; it is not a controlled comparison. The useful question survives: what institutions would let people create, trade and keep what they make?

Geography sets the limits, and people decide what to do within them. Jared Diamond, in Guns, Germs and Steel, stressed Africa's north-south shape; Thomas Sowell, the economist, stressed isolation. People advance by borrowing: Europeans crossed the Atlantic with Chinese rudders and counted with Indian numbers. Tropical Africa had less to borrow. The Sahara stood between it and the Mediterranean, its rivers fall a thousand feet through waterfalls on the way to the sea. The tsetse fly killed draft animals, and malaria killed people.

Isolation holds back any people. Russia had resources in abundance; what it lacked, Sowell wrote, "was not capital but the ability to use capital." The Soviet Union sat on some of the richest farmland on Earth and imported grain.

The rebuild question answers itself once you state it plainly. How do you reconstruct a territory still governed by the organizations that destroyed it? Hamas and Fatah kill Palestinians for opposing them. Dissidents are dragged through streets, thrown from buildings, shot in public. The leadership is a long line of men who trade in blood money and promise their foot soldiers a supernatural afterlife for the murder of civilians. Pour concrete and they tunnel under it. Send generators and they power weapons manufacturing. Open a border crossing and they smuggle rockets through it. Every functional state was built on the same prerequisite: remove the people whose power depends on perpetual failure.

The Abraham Accords showed that Arab governments and Israel could pursue normalization and cooperation instead of treating permanent hostility as fate. The agreements did not end every conflict or proceed identically in every country. They did open a path worth widening: make the gains from peace concrete.

Israel's Christian population has grown over the state's lifetime, while ancient Christian communities elsewhere in the region have suffered war, persecution and emigration. The causes and dates differ by country. The point is to see those communities too, rather than notice persecution only when it serves the preferred story.

Palestinians have also suffered under Arab governments: Jordan's Black September, expulsions from Kuwait, restrictions in Lebanon and the Syrian war. Jordan has also granted citizenship to many Palestinian refugees. The record differs by country. Those histories deserve attention without being used to cancel anyone else's obligations.

Palestinian factions have killed Palestinians accused of collaboration. Such killings belong in any account of intimidation and the price of dissent. The victim's life counts whoever kills him.

The Palestinian Authority's prisoner and family payments long drew criticism because amounts were linked to sentences, rewarding families of people convicted of attacks. In February 2025 Abbas announced a reform replacing that system with need-based assistance. Whether the payments actually changed requires implementation evidence. A declared reform should be tested as hard as the policy it claims to replace. 28

## The Institutions

International courts are not above scrutiny. The ICC's arrest warrants concern individual criminal responsibility; the ICJ case concerns a state's obligations under the Genocide Convention. The ICJ's provisional measures were not a final finding that genocide had occurred, nor an acquittal. Its 2024 advisory opinion on occupation is a different proceeding again. A serious criticism keeps the institutions, procedures and legal tests separate. 26

Deterrence depends on what a regime values and what it believes it can survive. Iran's repression and support for armed groups are reasons for vigilance. Theology matters to its politics; it does not prove every leader welcomes national suicide. A defense strategy must test the adversary's conduct, capabilities and incentives.

The United States' 2016 memorandum with Israel provided for $38 billion in military assistance over fiscal years 2019-2028, subject to congressional appropriation. Much of that spending supports American defense production; additional wartime appropriations are a separate count. Joint research and defense cooperation bring benefits. Whether those benefits justify a particular commitment is an investment question, not an accusation of dual loyalty. 28

The military return deserves an inventory: technology, intelligence, joint development and operational experience. So do the costs, dependencies and disagreements. Calling military assistance procurement does not make every dollar a measured return. Publish the balance sheet. The case for an ally should survive one.

The oldest institution in the dock is the church, and its case against the Jews began as self-defense. In 64 the emperor Nero blamed Christians for the fire of Rome and had them crucified or burned as torches, the Roman historian Tacitus wrote. So the Gospels lift the sentence off Pilate: in Luke he finds no fault in Jesus three times, and the crowds the priests feared too much to arrest Jesus in public suddenly cry, "Crucify him." While Christians were hunted, the story only shielded them. Once the emperors were Christian, it became law against the Jews. Yet one of Elaine Pagels's Harvard professors, a German Bible scholar who had served in Hitler's army, told her there is nothing anti-Jewish in the New Testament.

The title made the danger plain. Augustus, the first emperor, styled himself divi filius, son of the deified Julius Caesar, and his coins carried it; Tiberius's silver denarius, the likeliest coin in "Render therefore unto Caesar" (Matthew 22:21), names him son of the divine Augustus. In Greek it is simply son of god. Emperors had their signs too: in Alexandria, Vespasian healed a blind man with his spittle (Tacitus, Histories 4.81), as Jesus does at Bethsaida (Mark 8:23). The emperor's cult flourished in Asia Minor and around the Aegean, from Pergamum's temple to Rome and Augustus in 29 BCE to Ephesus and Smyrna, the cities of Paul's letters and Revelation's seven churches, and Revelation calls Pergamum the place "where Satan's seat is" (2:13). Mark opens with "the Son of God" and, at the cross, puts the title in a Roman centurion's mouth: "Truly this man was the Son of God" (15:39). John's Thomas calls Jesus "My Lord and my God" (20:28), the title Domitian demanded for himself, dominus et deus. A crucified "King of the Jews" hailed as son of god was Caesar's rival, and confessing him was an act of rebellion: around 112 Pliny the Younger, Rome's governor in Bithynia, freed accused Christians who offered incense to the emperor's image and cursed Christ.

James Carroll says reading the Gospels without the Roman war is like reading Anne Frank's diary without the Nazi roundups in Amsterdam. The war ran, on and off, from 66 to 135: Josephus counts 1.1 million dead in the siege of Jerusalem alone, and Cassius Dio 580,000 killed in the last revolt. It set Jew against Jew, and the Gospels record one side of the quarrel: where John writes "the Jews," Carroll says, read "those Jews who disagree with us Jews about Jesus." Christians forgot the war, and some forgot who fought it: a Boston College professor, Carroll recalls, told a student that the Romans killed Jesus, and the student concluded, "Well, then they were Jews."

Carroll followed the church's case against the Jews through sixteen centuries in Constantine's Sword (2001), titled after the cross the first Christian emperor carried into battle. Before him the catacombs show the fish, the shepherd and the loaves, and hardly a cross. The book opens at Auschwitz, where a great cross from a 1979 papal Mass was planted beside the camp: a sign of redemption to Catholics and, to Jews, one more Christian claim laid over Jewish death. After the Council of Nicaea in 325, Constantine cut Easter loose from Passover: "Let us then have nothing in common with the detestable Jewish crowd."

By the end of that century heretics and pagans faced conversion or death, and Ambrose of Milan, the most powerful bishop of his day, wanted the same for Jews: in 388 he shamed the emperor Theodosius out of making Christians rebuild a synagogue they had burned at Callinicum. Augustine, whom Ambrose had baptized, refused. He had Jews live on among Christians as witnesses, in Carroll's gloss: "Jews must be allowed to survive, but never to thrive." Their misery was to prove the church right, so Jewish thriving reads as a threat. About 429 Rome let the Jewish patriarchate lapse, the last office of Jewish self-rule until 1948. In 1096 crusaders bound for Jerusalem first massacred the thriving Jews of Speyer, Worms and Mainz; in Mainz parents killed their own children rather than see them baptized, and synagogues still say the laments for them. In 1215 the Fourth Lateran Council ordered Jews into distinctive dress, and badges followed.

Augustine deserves the rest of the record too. From Psalm 59, "Slay them not," he argued that Jews must not be killed, and for centuries that doctrine gave Jews a protected, if degraded, place in Latin Christendom. In his Confessions he pictured God holding all of time at once, every past and future present together, fifteen centuries before relativity drew the block universe.

In On Free Choice of the Will he argued that seven and three make ten for everyone who reasons, now and always, a truth no mind made and none can change. In the City of God he wrote that God made the world in six days because six is a perfect number (equal to the sum of its divisors 1, 2, and 3), and that six would stay perfect even if the six days had never happened. He took the eternal truth of number as a road to God. Matheism takes it as God.

In 1391 mobs across Spain drove tens of thousands of Jews to the font, and the church found it could not trust a forced conversion. Spain's blood-purity statutes, from 1449, made Jewishness a matter of blood that baptism could not wash out; the Inquisition, from 1478, hunted the converts' secret Judaism; and the Jesuits kept a bar on Jewish descent, five generations back, until 1946: contempt for a religion became contempt for a race. The secret Jew became the enemy within, Carroll argues, a parasite on the body of Christendom, the picture the Nazis would paint of the German folk. In 1492 Spain expelled its Jews. Martin Luther told Christians in 1543 to set the synagogues on fire; when they burned on Kristallnacht in 1938, on his birthday, a Lutheran bishop, Martin Sasse, reprinted Luther's words in celebration. Much of the church's defense of Jews in the Holocaust, Carroll notes, was a defense of the baptized; to Hitler, baptism changed nothing.

The turn came late. Angelo Roncalli, who as the Vatican's envoy in Istanbul during the war helped Jews escape, became Pope John XXIII and called the Second Vatican Council. The Vatican recognized Israel only in 1993. In 2000 John Paul II prayed at the Western Wall and left a note asking God's forgiveness for "those who in the course of history have caused these children of yours to suffer," which Carroll reads as the undoing of Augustine's two proofs, the scattering of the Jews and the ruin of their Temple. Yet in 2022 Carroll said he had naively thought, twenty years before, that the corner had been turned.

The truth stares them in the face of their own books, yet in supernatural lies, they lie to themselves and the world. In the fraudulent Gospel of John, written over sixty years after the events, Yeshua, a metaphor for salvation, was killed by Rome: like Neo defeats Agent Smith, salvation conquered Rome's demonic legions from within. Yet the dragon still lies on the one ring of power in the Vatican. Good Catholics feed it while praying it stays asleep. But today the Pope at times is a good leader of men, a shepherd for a flock of sheep, and in Nostra Aetate, at the Second Vatican Council, the church is making amends.

Amends for its ancient original sin, antisemitism: inverting and projecting a flipped reality onto the people who gave it its God, until it led to the Holocaust. Supersessionism comes from the Latin for sitting upon: the church sits upon the synagogue. Once the church had replaced Israel, Carroll says, Israel had no religious reason left to exist, and down the centuries that became no physical reason to exist, epitomized at Auschwitz. The Nazis did something new, Carroll told an audience at New York's 92nd Street Y in 2002, but they could not have done it without the ground the church prepared, and Pope Pius XII's silence during the Holocaust "is not the crime, it's the evidence." In 1965 Nostra Aetate ruled that Christ's passion "cannot be charged against all the Jews, without distinction, then alive, nor against the Jews of today," and that "God holds the Jews most dear for the sake of their Fathers." Carroll calls it the most important change in Christian theology ever. Even so, it keeps the Gospels' charge that "the Jewish authorities and those who followed their lead pressed for the death of Christ," and still calls the church "the new people of God." In 2015 the Vatican's commission for relations with the Jews wrote that the church "neither conducts nor supports any specific institutional mission work directed towards Jews."

Yet there's also a time for the real truth to be revealed: that math is God and all is one, and that what a single man can correct, a single man can lead into devastation when he engages in politics from the seat of the one ring of power, which is wrapped around a supernatural lie. The salvation of Yeshua is in his monist teaching, in the Gospel of Thomas, that Matheism completes.

The oldest European complaint resurfaces in America: Jews hold too much power, and the power is not in America's interest. The charge deserves a direct answer. What are the powerful Jews in America guilty of? Helping to build American strength in medicine, technology, mathematics, science and business? Judge the work and the individual. BOOK 6 Tribe of Truth vs Consensus

## Tribe of Truth

Most people hate the truth. They love tribalism while pretending to love the truth. If you must be tribal, then let us be the tribe of truth. Alan Watts, the British writer who popularized Zen in the West, drew the line in 1951: belief clings to what one wishes were true, and faith opens the mind to the truth, whatever it turns out to be. This book holds every side to that standard: the government that lied, the experts who agree, the podcasters who sell the counterclaim, and you. Every tribe will meet a truth it hates. Beneath all their fights, the floor is math.

A Sufi teaching story, told by the writer Idries Shah and retold by Robert Greene, author of The 48 Laws of Power: one day the water changes, and everyone who drinks it goes mad. One man stores clean water and stays sane, and alone. In the end he drinks with the rest, to belong. The tribe of truth keeps the clean water, and drinks it together.

People can be steered without a room where everyone receives an order. You watch who gets funded, published and promoted, and who gets shown the door. You learn where the bread is buttered. The people who read the signal best rise fastest. From above it can look like an army under command, although most participants were never given one. In A Few Good Men, they called it a Code Red. No written order. Everyone below understood what was expected.

Authority transfer and bias: A credential in one field is presented as evidence in another. A physicist endorsing an economic policy. An intelligence official weighing in on a laptop. The authority is real. Its relevance is manufactured. Intelligence officials are not considered above lying for "national security," which often means security of their jobs, their reputation after a media manufactured hit, or political allies they would betray with the truth.

Asymmetric skepticism: Extraordinary evidence is demanded for one side; the other side's claims pass on repetition. You can't prove the lab did it beside we know it came from a wet market, without equivalent scrutiny. Calling something a conspiracy theory does the work of a rebuttal without providing one. Real conspiracies exist. Their existence does not validate an unrelated accusation.

Russian collusion was claimed for years, based on a fabricated Steele Dossier, paid for by Hillary Clinton's campaign and the Democratic National Committee, that alleged a golden shower prank and referenced a Russian consulate in Miami that does not exist. The Federal Election Commission fined them $113,000 for passing off the payments as legal fees.

Burden shifting: The person asking the question is required to prove the negative. The one making the claim and demanding the policy bears a burden too. Faster development can draw on prior work, overlapping trials and large funding; it does not make long-term follow-up unnecessary. Show what was measured, for how long, and which uncertainties remain.

Motte-and-bailey: A modest, defensible claim is swapped with a much stronger desired conclusion. Humans affect climate becomes we must adopt this particular economic program. Challenge the second and the retreat is to the first.

Social proof cascade: Once enough prominent voices repeat a claim, the repetition itself becomes the evidence. "Everyone agrees" is not data. It is pressure.

Credential gatekeeping: Only credentialed voices count, and the credentials are issued by the institutions whose claims are in question. The barber judges whether you need a haircut.

Pathos over logos: The Greeks identified three ways to persuade: logos, by reason and evidence; ethos, by the speaker's character; and pathos, by feeling. James Lindsay, the critic of woke ideology, argues that woke persuades by the last two: a false ethic that casts its side as the only good people, held in place by stirred-up emotion, until enough repetition makes it feel like consensus.

## The Climate Cult: A Criticism

The 97% became a marketing number. Cook and colleagues' 2013 study classified 11,944 abstracts; 97.1 percent of the 4,014 expressing a position endorsed human-caused warming, explicitly or implicitly. In the narrower category explicitly quantifying humans as the main cause, the study counted 64. Legates and colleagues disputed that classification and counted 41, about 0.34 percent of all abstracts. Different claim. Different denominator. The 41 is not the number of scientists who accept human influence, and abstracts expressing no position are not votes against it. Neither percentage decides the projected damage or the best policy. 20

In 2009 Doran and Zimmerman invited 10,257 Earth scientists and received 3,146 responses. Their specialist subgroup contained 79 people; 77 answered the question about human influence, and 75 agreed it was a significant contributing factor. That is the source of 97.4 percent. A selected expert subgroup is not a poll of every invited scientist, and significant contribution is not a forecast of catastrophe or an economic case for a particular policy. 21

7,111 invitees did not respond. Silence is not agreement. It is not disagreement either. Expertise, selection and nonresponse belong in the interpretation of a survey.

Ice-age records show that temperature and CO2 interact; the initial driver of a glacial transition and its amplifying feedbacks need not be the same thing. CO2 can lag an initial temperature change and then add warming. Today's fossil-carbon input is a different initiating perturbation. The historical graph does not by itself tell us the cost of a future degree, and a film's rhetoric should not be mistaken for that calculation. 22

The person who promotes a consensus study may also run a site explaining it. That is a reason to inspect the underlying work, not a reason the explanation must be false. Test the claim itself.

You do not need a grand conspiracy to produce conformity. You need a paycheck and a crowd to follow at lunch. A grant funds your lab, your students, your next paper. Careers grow inside a paradigm. Threaten the paradigm and you threaten what has been built on it. Those incentives deserve scrutiny in public research and in the industries that oppose it. Neither side's funding establishes its conclusion.

Climate scientist Judith Curry retired from Georgia Tech in 2017 and publicly criticized the politicization and professional incentives she had encountered. Her account belongs in the discussion of how dissent is treated. The next question is whether a specific dissenting claim survives the evidence. 22

Show the warming. Show the attribution, how much of it humans caused. Show the projected harm. Show what the remedy costs and what it achieves. Each claim carries its own burden. A percentage does not carry any of them.

## The Science Has Its Own Questions

A forecast of catastrophe is not a theorem. It depends on emissions, physical response, exposure, adaptation and the value assigned to damage. Greenhouse warming has evidence behind it; a particular prediction of social collapse requires more. The burden is to show the steps.

CO2 forcing is approximately logarithmic: each doubling adds roughly the same forcing over the relevant range. That does not make the effect stop. The warming after feedbacks is another question. The IPCC's 2021 assessment gave an equilibrium sensitivity best estimate of 3 degrees Celsius per doubling, with a likely range of 2.5 to 4. Critics who argue for a lower response must challenge the feedback evidence, not merely point to the logarithm. 22

Life existed under far higher ancient CO2 levels. That is a fact about life's range, not a guarantee that today's cities, crops and ecosystems can absorb any rate of change without cost. Ancient geography, sunlight and ocean chemistry differed. Ask what the comparison controls before asking it to settle the present.

Cloud feedbacks remain an important uncertainty. Some clouds cool by reflecting sunlight; others warm by affecting outgoing radiation. Water vapor is also a feedback. Models approximate these processes, and observations constrain them. Show which uncertainty changes the conclusion, by how much, instead of treating either a model or the word uncertainty as an answer. 22

Temperature records need adjustments for station moves, observation times and changing instruments. An adjustment should be reproducible, its effect visible, and its rationale open to challenge. The direction of an adjustment alone does not establish fraud. Compare independent records and methods, not two graphs stripped of their definitions.

Fear mongering sells, especially in politics. The enemies of peace through strength for Pax Americana, the American-led peace, beat their drums against fear mongering, except when it's climate hysteria.

## The Institutions Behind the Science

Climate catastrophism is the secular religion, the newest form of an old one: for thousands of years people have blamed catastrophe on human sin and paid to hold it off, from the witches Europe burned for hail and frost, to the hearts the Aztecs cut out to keep the sun rising, back past the biblical flood to the Epic of Gilgamesh. Apocalypse: the world ends unless you repent. Priesthood: credentialed experts whose authority must not be questioned. Heresy: denial, the word itself borrowed from theology. Indulgences: carbon credits, purchased so the buyer may keep sinning while the peasant cannot. And like any religion, the first thing it forbids is the question that would test it.

A carbon offset is a medieval indulgence: the sin is committed, a payment is made, and the sinner is absolved. The payment does not undo the emission. The market for offsets has been shown repeatedly to include credits for forests that were never going to be cut, reductions that were already happening, and double-counted savings. The structure is identical: the institution that defines the sin sells the forgiveness.

Nuclear is a proven, scalable low-carbon energy source. It is opposed by the same environmental organizations claiming a climate emergency. France generates roughly 70 percent of its electricity from nuclear and has among the lowest carbon emissions per kilowatt-hour in Europe. Germany shut its nuclear plants. Emissions fell anyway on wind and solar, but keeping nuclear would have displaced more fossil fuel. One country followed the science. The other followed the movement. To Putin's delight, Germany relied on Russian energy, like Trump said, and he was mocked for it. The truth makes grifters nervously join the useful idiots. There are trillions of dollars at stake. Russia, China, Qatar and other dictatorships are highly incentivized to prevent other nations from developing their own fossil fuel industries.

A politically uniform department can miss questions its members do not think to ask. Peer review is a filter run by people, not a theorem that the result is true. Independent methods, open data and adversarial examination are ways to catch what a shared prior lets through.

Climate policy creates constituencies: renewable-energy companies, carbon traders, consultants, regulators and researchers. Fossil-fuel production has constituencies too. Follow the incentives. Then examine the measurements and compare the policies.

## The Cost of the Cure

Billions of people depend on reliable energy for food production, water treatment, heating, cooling, transport and medical care. Remove it without a replacement at scale and you do not save them. The question is how to reduce damage without destroying the capacity that keeps people alive. Compare feasible replacements, infrastructure, reliability and cost, not an ideal future with a real present.

It included guaranteed employment, universal healthcare, affordable housing. So did the USSR, Cuba, Venezuela, and North Korea. Follow the data of economic reality: wealth is not created but destroyed by what amounts to communism. A restructuring of the economy that had nothing to do with carbon. The reddest program in the greenest coat.

Asked whether she would support a carbon tax instead, Ocasio-Cortez's chief of staff, Saikat Chakrabarti, told a reporter in 2019 that the Green New Deal was not really about climate: "it wasn't originally a climate thing at all... we really think of it as a how-do-you-change-the-entire-economy thing."

Modern Monetary Theory says a sovereign can't run out of its own currency. True, but irrelevant: equal spending on a bridge and a bonfire produces different inflation, because what the money builds determines whether prices rise.

## Ask the Questions

Ask why Einstein and Galileo didn't need a consensus. Ask if consensus is the gold standard of the scientific method or of politics. The consensus, the framing, the repetition, the certainty are not scientific method or truth. Discussion can be good. But truth doesn't need a committee. It's rather warped by interested parties. Part of respecting each other as the image of reason is to heed the call to discern and expect it of our neighbors.

1. Reef cover can recover after losses and fall again after bleaching or storms. Which regions, dates and measures are being compared, and what happened after the record?

2. A tide gauge measures local relative sea level. What do the local record, land motion and global satellite record each show?

3. Storm frequency, intensity, rainfall and damage are different measures. Which changed, and what contribution comes from people building in exposed places?

4. Cold and heat both kill. How do adaptation, population aging and future warming change each risk? A historical ratio is not a forecast.

5. Which Arctic forecast was actually made, by whom, with what probability and definition of ice-free? Was it a prediction or a worst-case scenario?

6. An island building airports is evidence of investment. Is its flood risk rising, falling or being managed? What does the engineering cost?

7. Polar bear estimates depend on the population, year and survey method. Do not turn an uncertain 1960s baseline into a precise global trend.

8. CO2 fertilization can increase plant growth, with limits from water, nutrients and heat. Where does greening outweigh other damage, and where does it not?

9. Falling disaster mortality shows the value of wealth, warning systems and adaptation. How much did each contribute, and what should that teach policy?

10. Crop yields reflect technology as well as weather. Compare the observed yield with the yield under the alternative climate and the same technology.

11. Regional medieval warmth does not by itself establish a globally synchronous temperature above today’s. What do the geographically distributed records show?

12. Remove a failed glacier prediction from a sign, then explain why it failed. A correction should be visible, not quietly forgotten.

13. The 1970s literature did not show a scientific consensus for global cooling. A memorable cover is not a count of the research.

14. Global burned area, forest fire severity and damage to settlements need separate counts. A fall in one does not decide the others.

15. The Netherlands demonstrates that engineering can manage major water risk. Compare that protection’s cost with the damage avoided and the capacity to sustain it.

16. Some atolls have gained land area while sea level rose. Land area, elevation, fresh water and habitability are different measurements.

17. A long gap between US major hurricane landfalls does not, by itself, measure the trend in global storms. Choose the comparison before seeing the answer.

18. Tree-ring proxies and instrument records can diverge. Show the splice, the uncertainty and the reason for each reconstruction choice.

19. A warming world can still have record snowfalls. What does the longer record say about snowfall, snow cover and extremes?

20. Drought and floods can occur in the same region at different times. Which forecast was falsified, and which variable was it actually predicting?

21. Tropical upper-air trends are a test of observations and models. Compare consistent datasets, heights and periods; the phrase missing hot spot is not the analysis.

22. Antarctic sea ice reached a high in 2014 and severe lows later. An argument should survive the later data as well as the selected year.

23. Where the Sahara’s margins green, what caused it? Rainfall, land use and CO2 need to be separated rather than assumed.

24. Which published forecasts have performed well and which have failed? Score the original forecasts, including the emissions they assumed.

25. How much does each climate dollar change emissions, resilience and human welfare, compared with the best alternative use?

Ask the questions of the consensus and of its critics. A correction should improve the argument, not change the subject. The pandemic tested the same habits in months instead of decades.

## How Trust Was Broken

Anthony Fauci ran NIAID, the National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases. Francis Collins ran NIH, the National Institutes of Health. Peter Daszak ran EcoHealth. NIAID's money reached the Wuhan Institute of Virology through EcoHealth. This was an authorized program, with named leaders and public funding.

Wuhan researchers made modified SARS-related coronaviruses and tested their ability to infect cells carrying the human receptor, the protein these viruses latch onto. That work was published. The question is who authorized the risks, who judged the safeguards sufficient, and who was responsible for checking the results. "Oversight failure" describes what went wrong. It does not establish why. Negligence, institutional self-protection, and deliberate avoidance of scrutiny are different explanations. Calling it a failure does not automatically establish the innocent one.

## The Research Was Real

A 2015 paper described a laboratory-made SARS-like coronavirus able to infect human airway cells. You do not have to establish that this particular virus became SARS-CoV-2 to ask who authorized the risk and who would answer for an accident.

The Obama administration paused federal funding for certain gain-of-function experiments (work that gives viruses new abilities) in 2014. That was a funding policy with a defined scope, not a criminal statute banning every experiment bearing the name. The 2023 audit by the inspector general of Health and Human Services found ineffective oversight. NIH had not referred the research for the additional outside review, and it failed to follow up a progress report until nearly two years after the report was due. When the report arrived, NIH concluded that the viral-growth findings should have triggered immediate reporting. The safeguard was on paper. The follow-through was missing.

Fauci's sweeping denial to Congress that NIH funded gain-of-function research in Wuhan rested on the government's narrower definition. Rand Paul challenged that distinction. Whatever label qualifies for a particular review, the public question remains: what capabilities were researchers giving the viruses, and who checked the work? A definition cannot disinfect a laboratory.

## Saint Fauci

"I represent science," Fauci said. That is the inversion in a single line. Science is the standing permission to be proven wrong out loud; the hour a man becomes the science, doubt turns to heresy and the method is already dead. The line attributed to Louis XIV is "I am the state." Palpatine says he is the Senate. A lab coat saying as much of truth is the same claim in a humbler costume.

Fauci became a public symbol, praised by one camp and treated as a villain by another. The scientific question is what he knew, what he said, and which decisions the record supports. Celebrity does not answer it.

The January 2020 emails show scientists discussing a possible laboratory connection before that possibility became a public test of political loyalty. A change of view can be justified. The record should show the reasoning, and disagreements inside the room should not be hidden from the people asked to trust the result.

On July 29, 2026, Fauci appeared before the Senate and repeatedly invoked the Fifth Amendment. That is his right, not a confession. The official transcript records both the questions and his refusal to answer them. He said he believed the proceeding was aimed at prosecution rather than fact-finding. 25

Biden issued Fauci a preemptive federal pardon in January 2025. A pardon is not a finding of guilt or innocence. It also does not automatically erase every possible basis for invoking the Fifth Amendment: its scope, state exposure and other unpardoned conduct can matter. The public can still demand records and explanations. 25

Clemency can protect someone from a feared political prosecution. It can also frustrate accountability. Judge the evidence of misconduct independently of the pardon, and demand an explanation for the breadth of the protection.

A pardon takes prison off the table. It does not take the truth off the table. If the point of clemency is mercy rather than concealment, the natural bargain is the one nobody offered: you will not be imprisoned, and you will answer.

## Pardons and Corruption

Biden pardoned Hunter and later issued preemptive pardons to family members. Hunter's foreign business dealings and the access associated with a famous surname deserved scrutiny. A payment to an associated entity, however, is not proof of a payment to every person named alongside it. State the recipient, the transaction and the evidence. 25

The pardon pen writes for both parties. Trump pardoned allies and political associates too. A standard that excuses your side's conflicts while prosecuting the other's is tribal loyalty pretending to be law. Corruption has no party.

A search warrant is not a conviction. An indictment is not a conviction. A dismissal can concern procedure rather than factual innocence. Apply those distinctions to political enemies as readily as friends. Each side's raid must not become permission for the other's revenge.

## The Emails and the Takedown

On January 31, 2020, Kristian Andersen emailed Fauci that unusual features of the virus suggested possible engineering. By February 4, Andersen was arguing against that explanation. The published Proximal Origin paper came on March 17. It rejected engineering and went further, dismissing any laboratory-based scenario as implausible. A change of mind can be earned. Show the evidence that earned that much certainty.

The Great Barrington Declaration supplies a different, more direct record. Its principal authors were Martin Kulldorff of Harvard, Sunetra Gupta of Oxford and Jay Bhattacharya of Stanford. They argued for focused protection of the vulnerable instead of broad lockdowns. In an October 8, 2020 email to Fauci, NIH director Francis Collins called them fringe epidemiologists and requested a "quick and devastating published take down" of their premises. Fauci sent him published attacks. Three distinguished universities, suddenly fringe when their scientists challenged the policy.

Scott Atlas, who served on the White House coronavirus task force, describes bringing contrary evidence into meetings with Fauci, Deborah Birx and other officials and encountering resistance instead of an open examination of the data. That is his account of the room. Collins's email gives us the official's own words outside it. Follow the science, provided it follows us.

David Morens, Fauci's senior adviser, wrote about making emails disappear. In correspondence released by the House

COVID subcommittee, he discussed using private email and wrote that he had learned how "to make emails disappear" during the public-records process. The communications concerned, among other matters, his exchanges with Peter Daszak. What was deleted? What was retained? Which official business moved into private channels?

Masks exposed another confusion between evidence and managing the public. In March 2020 Fauci discouraged general public masking; by June he advocated it and acknowledged that conserving scarce protective equipment had shaped the earlier message. Was yesterday's certainty wrong, or was it a tool to make people behave? Either way, stop treating today's certainty as a commandment.

## Conflicts, Censorship and Conformity

Peter Daszak co-authored the February 2020 Lancet statement condemning suggestions of an unnatural origin as conspiracy theories. His organization collaborated with the Wuhan laboratory under scrutiny. In June 2021, The Lancet published an expanded disclosure of his interests. In January 2025 HHS debarred EcoHealth and Daszak for five years.

Mark Zuckerberg acknowledged government pressure to censor COVID content. In his August 26, 2024 letter to Congress, he said senior Biden administration officials, including the White House, repeatedly pressured Meta, even over humor and satire. He called the pressure wrong, regretted not speaking out more, and accepted responsibility for Meta's decisions.

He also described an FBI warning about a possible Russian information operation involving the Biden family and Burisma. When the New York Post published its Hunter Biden story,

Facebook reduced its distribution while awaiting review. Zuckerberg acknowledged that the reporting was not Russian disinformation and that demoting it was a mistake. A warning from the state had helped prepare the platform to distrust a real story.

Fifty-one former intelligence officials, not fifty-one agencies, added their authority in an October 2020 letter invoking the hallmarks of a Russian information operation. The letter itself admitted that they lacked evidence of Russian involvement. The credentials were real. They were not evidence about the laptop. Borrowed authority did the work that verification had not done.

Some of Trump's mocked claims came out partly true. In March 2017 he tweeted that Obama had his "wires tapped" in Trump Tower before the election, and he was widely mocked. The Justice Department found no record of such wiretaps. Yet from October 2016 the FBI had kept Carter Page, who had just left the campaign as a foreign policy adviser, under surveillance on four court orders, and in December 2019 the Justice Department's inspector general found seventeen significant errors and omissions in the applications; an FBI lawyer had altered an email used to renew one. The inspector general found no political bias in opening the investigation. In 2023 the special counsel John Durham faulted the FBI for opening it on "raw, unanalyzed, and uncorroborated intelligence."

The Twitter Files exposed restrictions users could not see. Matt Taibbi's congressional testimony described an internal record placing Stanford physician Jay Bhattacharya on a "trends blacklist." A scientist challenging pandemic policy could speak while the platform restricted his visibility. Elon Musk's purchase of Twitter enabled the release of those records. Meta ended its US third-party fact-checking program in January 2025. Two companies, two reversals, in the same direction. The decisions were not coordinated by a conspiracy.

They were coordinated by the same incentive once the incentive changed.

In Murthy v. Missouri, the Supreme Court decided that the plaintiffs lacked standing, the legal right to bring the case; it did not decide the underlying First Amendment merits. The record still needs examining. Pressure the gatekeeper to hide the disputed speech, and you have changed who is allowed to hear the argument.

## The Rehearsal and the Timeline

Wuhan's research records and databases matter to investigating origins. Missing access leaves questions unanswered. It does not establish what the missing records contain. The demand is access to the evidence.

On October 18, 2019, Johns Hopkins, the World Economic Forum and the Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation held a fictional coronavirus-pandemic exercise called Event 201. Its recommendations called for governments, business and media to coordinate messaging, prioritize authoritative accounts and suppress false ones. The organizers explicitly described the scenario as fictional, not a prediction of COVID-19. Its timing is not evidence of foreknowledge. Who decides which is which? Who examines the authorities when their own funding, judgments and reputations are at stake?

At the World Economic Forum in Davos in 2023, Moderna CEO Stephane Bancel recalled the company's 100,000 doses in 2019 and asking his manufacturing chief for a billion the next year: "We need to make a billion doses next year, there's going to be a pandemic." Ten thousand fold. His separately dated account puts his pandemic alarm at Davos in January 2020.

Moderna's vaccine timeline has dates. The viral sequence was shared on January 11, 2020, NIH and Moderna finalized their vaccine sequence on January 13, and the first clinical batch was completed on February 7.

A short sequence match between a viral genome and a patent sequence needs a properly specified comparison: which sequences, which orientations, how many searches and what background model? A probability computed after finding the match can badly overstate its surprise. The match alone does not establish that the virus was engineered or that a company knew it was coming.

Jon Stewart made the absurdity into a joke on Colbert in June 2021: a coronavirus outbreak in a city with a laboratory studying coronaviruses, and people acted as though asking about the laboratory was unthinkable.
